And what has not

What Has Been Read

A search that found nothing is still a result: it stops the next person repeating it. This page records both what has been done and what is still outstanding.

FindMyPast — a six-day trial, spent

What a paid subscription was actually worth here

The trial expires on 16 September 2026, so it was swept systematically rather than browsed. It is recorded in full, negatives included — the most useful thing a search can do for the next person is stop them repeating it, and two of the negatives below would otherwise cost someone a subscription to discover.

found
Serafina Arena — baptism — Baptised 1898, OUR LADY OF LORETTO, New York County
found
Clementina Arena — baptism — Baptised 1901, OUR LADY OF LORETTO, New York County
retracted
Clementina Arena's 1921 crossing — WITHDRAWN. IT IS SOMEBODY ELSE'S. — US/PASS/NY/ELLIS2/000191616/3 is a different Clementina Arena.
found
Generoso Arena — a crossing in 1903 — Ellis Island, 1903, aged about 9 (b. 1894)
corroborated
Prostamo in the Australian passenger index — Five records: Caterina 1969, Domenico F 1956, Francesca 1957, Francesco 1957, Rocco 1949
negative
Rocco Arena in the 1910 US census — Not there
negative
Italy Births & Baptisms 1806-1900, and Marriages, and Deaths — DOES NOT COVER SCILLA
negative
Italy Marriages 1809-1900 for Rosario Mazza — Four Rosario Mazza marriages, none after 1862
negative
Queensland Marriages 1829-1939 — Ends too early
found
THE LOCK IS OPEN — the New York Roman Catholic parish transcripts, read — The full subscription opens them. FOUR ARENA BAPTISMS at Our Lady of Loretto naming ROCCO ARENA and GENEROSA COSTA, with the Mulberry Street addresses; the Arlotta children; and the marriage register, from which two Arena namesakes were excluded on their parents' names.
method
The signed-out session, resolved — and why a lapsed subscription is not a negative — Access restored 16 September 2026 when the account holder took a full month's subscription and signed in. Everything this row listed as wanted has now been run.
found
Rocco Arlotta — THE 1900 LODGER, IDENTIFIED — Married ANTONIETTA TAVANI, 4 June 1900, Manhattan. Aged 22, born 1878. Father ANTONIO ARLOTTA, mother NICOLINA CARATOZZOLA.
found
Our Lady of Loretto — the Scilla colony in one Manhattan parish — Eighty-four Arena baptisms in the New York Roman Catholic parish index, with the father's given name against each.
retracted
RETRACTED — the godparents are not behind that paywall, because they are not in the record at all — The full transcript has now been read. ITS FIELDS ARE: record set, name, birth year, baptism year, parish, county, state, country, MOTHER'S first and last name, FATHER'S first and last name, birth date, baptism date, location, archdiocese, RESIDENCE, language. THERE IS NO GODPARENT FIELD, and there is no «View original record» button -- these baptisms are TRANSCRIPT ONLY, with no image.
negative
Rocco Arena in the 1910 US census — confirmed absent from a second index — Zero results.
negative
Domenica Prostamo's 1951 voyage — absent from a SECOND index — Australia, Inward, Outward & Coastal Passenger Lists 1826-1972 holds five Prostamo and she is not among them.
negative
The Australian Mazza sets do not reach this family — Queensland parish burials hold two Mazza, neither ours.
negative
Rosa Arena and Clementina Arena of Vittoria — a different family — Giuseppe Verdi out of Naples, 1921. Rosa 27, b.1894, resident VITTORIA (Ragusa), father GIOVANNI; Clementina entered as her sister.
found
Rocco Arena's four crossings — all four now named, dated and with a ship — 1892 ELYSIA ex Naples, aged 31; 1894 SUEVIA ex Naples, aged 33; 1900 TRAVE ex Genoa, aged 39; 1913 SAN GUGLIELMO ex Messina, aged 52. Indexed birth year 1861 on every one.
found
The 1913 crossing names his wife and a nephew — San Guglielmo out of Messina, 1913. Rocco Arena, 52, US Citizen. DEPARTURE PORT RELATION (WIFE): CORTA GENEROSA. ARRIVAL PORT RELATION (NEPHEW): ANTONIO ARENA.
corroborated
Anna Arena's 1899 crossing — a second index agrees — Archimede ex Napoli, 1899. Anna Arena, age 6, birth year 1893, RESIDENCE SCILLA.
negative
The other Giovanni Arena of 1899 is from Serra San Bruno — «Gmi. Giovanni Antonio Arena», 41, b.1858, ship Bolivia ex Naples, 1899. RESIDENCE: SERRA S. BRUNO. Arrival port relation (BROTHER-IN-LAW): Vincenzo Pisano.
negative
Giuseppe Arena of Scilla — no American crossing indexed — Zero results for Giuseppe Arena with Scilla in the travel and migration records.
found
Rocco Arlotta's children name his parents back — three for three — Our Lady of Loretto: NICOLINA b.1901, ANTONIO b.1903, PAPPINA (Peppina) b.1904, father ROCCO in each.
negative
Rocco Arena has no naturalisation petition in this collection — Zero matching records; the Rocco Arena entries are born 1896 and petition in 1942-44.
negative
Antonio Arena, born New York 1896, is not in the New York draft cards — The New York WWI registrations hold an Antonio Arena born 1876 and an «Antonio Rena» Arena born 1886. Neither is 1896.
found
ANTONIO ARENA'S BAPTISM — and the Mulberry Street address, confirmed — Born 3 OCTOBER 1896, baptised 15 NOVEMBER 1896 at OUR LADY OF LORETTO, Manhattan. Father ROCCO ARENA, mother GENEROSA COSTA. RESIDENCE: 141 MULBERRY STREET.
found
THE WHOLE NEW YORK HOUSEHOLD — four children, two addresses, one infant death — Antonio b. 3 Oct 1896 (141 Mulberry); Serafina b. 15 Aug 1898 (141 Mulberry); Clementina b. 21 Dec 1900 (198 Mulberry); Antonio b. 13 Oct 1902 (Most Precious Blood). All «father Rocco Arena, mother Generosa Costa».
negative
Joannem Arena, married at Our Lady of Loretto in 1899, is a Palermo family and not ours — Groom's father THOMAE ARENA, mother ANGELICAE COGLITORE, residence PALERMO; bride IGNATIAM ARNATO of POLIZZI GENEROSA, daughter of Vincentii Arnato and Losephae Fiorito. Married 17 December 1899.
found
SEBASTIANO MAZZA ON THE 1959 QUEENSLAND ELECTORAL ROLL — Progress Road, Wacol — Sebastiano Mazza, male, BUILDER'S LABOURER, Progress Road, WACOL, sub-district INALA, district McPherson, Queensland, 1959 roll, number 3779. The only Sebastiano Mazza in the entire collection.
found
ROSARIO AND ANGELA MAZZA AT 225 LEICHHARDT STREET — one address, and a correction to how it was read — ROSARIO MAZZA, SHOPKEEPER, number 1845; ANGELA MAZZA, HOME DUTIES, number 1844. Both at 225 Leichhardt Street, City, Brisbane, 1959 roll.
found
ROSARIO SALVATORE MAZZA, 1949 — 191 Given Terrace, Paddington, shop assistant — Rosario Salvatore Mazza, male, SHOP ASSISTANT, 191 Given Terrace, PADDINGTON, Brisbane, Queensland, 1949 roll, number 4788.
found
The other Queensland Mazza of 1959 — Tully and Yelarbon, two trades, one of them a lead — ANDREA MAZZA, TIMBER CUTTER, Cardwell, sub-district TULLY, district Herbert, number 1422. ANTONIO MAZZA, TOBACCO GROWER, Wyenio, YELARBON, sub-district Inglewood, district Maranoa, number 857.
negative
DOMENICA PROSTAMO IS NOT ON ANY AUSTRALIAN ELECTORAL ROLL FINDMYPAST HOLDS — under either name — «Domenica Mazza» returns ZERO in the collection. «Prostamo» returns ZERO across the whole of Australia, every state and every year.
method
What Findmypast's «Australia Electoral Rolls» actually covers for Queensland — eight years, ending 1959 — QUEENSLAND: commonwealth rolls for 1903, 1906, 1913, 1922, 1934, 1941, 1949 and 1959 only, plus state rolls 1860-1884 and 1895-1915. 45% have images. Other states are thinner still: Western Australia 1939/1943/1949, Tasmania 1916/1934/1943, South Australia 1939/1941/1943, New South Wales 1903/1913/1935.
method
The Findmypast results URL silently corrupts searches when a parameter is wrong — in two different ways — VERIFIED PARAMETERS: datasetname, lastname, firstname, and the sort pair o= and d=. UNRECOGNISED ONES DO ONE OF TWO THINGS. «state=Queensland» and «page=2» render a filter chip and report «0 results found». «street=progress road» is discarded in silence and the unfiltered count comes back unchanged.
found
THE CRUPI OF TULLY — seven of them on one page of the 1959 roll, with trades and addresses — Numbers 504-510 in the Tully sub-district: ANTONIO, cane farmer, Dean Street; FRANCESCO, mechanic, Innisfail Road; GIOVANNA, home duties, Feluga; MARIA, home duties, no address; MARIO, lorry driver, no address; NELLIE, home duties, Dean Street; PIETRO, lorry driver, Birkala via Tully.
found
The Crupi a decade earlier, and the trades that changed — 1949 — ANTONIO CRUPI, BOOTMAKER, Bryant Street, Tully. MARIO CRUPI, BUS DRIVER, Mena Creek, sub-district INNISFAIL. A SECOND ANTONIO CRUPI, FARMER, Silkwood East, Innisfail, on the 1959 roll — a different man from the Tully cane farmer.
negative
POLISTENA and ROCCO ARENA are on no Australian electoral roll Findmypast holds — «Polistena» returns ZERO across the whole of Australia. «Rocco Arena» returns ZERO. The surname Arena returns 172, none of them this family: the Queensland Arena are a Cloncurry family enrolled from 1898 to 1934.
negative
MELLUSO and ANILE — nothing for either in Australia — «Melluso» returns ZERO across all of Australia. «Anile» returns TWO, both in WESTERN AUSTRALIA on the 1949 roll: Concetta Anile and Rosario Anile.
found
TWO SURNAME CLUSTERS IN QUEENSLAND THAT THIS ARCHIVE HAS NOT OPENED — Donato and Nicotra — DONATO: 65 results, with a Queensland block including CANDELORE DONATO (1941, 1949), CANDO DONATO (1939, 1959), DOMENICO DONATO (1959), ANNUNZIATO, CONCETTA and DOMINICO. NICOTRA: 39 results, almost all Queensland — FILIPPO (1934-1959), GREGORIO (1939-1959), GIUSEPPE, CARMELA, LEONARDA and LEONARD (1934-1959).
method
Two things that make a Findmypast sweep cheap — and one bad inference they exposed — javascript_tool WORKS on both the results page and the transcript page, returning every row with its /transcript?id= href in ONE page load, and the full transcript as plain text. The rolls are ordered ALPHABETICALLY BY GIVEN NAME within a surname within a sub-district.
negative
THE UGOLINO VIVALDI CANDIDATE — Findmypast could not test it, and the manifest then killed it — «Domenico Mazza», 1951, arrival 01 MAR 1951, ship UGOLINO VIVALDI, Genoa to Australia, BARCODE 30133197 — the identical NAA barcode this archive already carries. No age, no sex, no birth year, no other field the index did not already give.
method
For twentieth-century Australian arrivals, Findmypast IS the NAA index — not a second source — Every post-1945 row in «Australia, Inward, Outward & Coastal Passenger Lists 1826-1972» carries an id of the form ANZ/AUSPASS/NAA/xxxxxxxxx, a NAA BARCODE, and a link to recordsearch.naa.gov.au. The pre-1940 rows are different material — ANZ/VCPL and ANZ/MIGR/VICTORIAPASSLIST, the Victorian inward, outward and coastal lists.
found
ROCCO PROSTAMO, 10 August 1949 — a Prostamo crossing this archive has never held — Mr Rocco Prostamo, arrival 10 AUG 1949, ship NAPOLI, Genoa to MELBOURNE, Victoria. NAA barcode 9245827.
method
The year filter on Findmypast is «eventyear», and «year» is one of the parameters that lies — «year=1898» returns ZERO on a search whose unfiltered form plainly contains 1898 rows. «eventyear=1898» returns TWELVE. The sort keys, read out of the page's own column headers, are firstname, lastname, yearofbirth, EVENTYEAR, departureport and destinationport.
found
ROCCO ARENA ENTERS THE AMERICAN CENSUS ONCE, IN 1900, AND NEVER APPEARS AGAIN — 1900: EXACTLY ONE Rocco Arena in the whole United States — born 1861, Italy, New York — and he is ours. 1910: ZERO. 1920: two, born 1883 and 1913. 1930: six, the earliest born 1882. None of the later ones can be him.
negative
The naturalisation is still not found, and the census now says where to look for it — No Rocco Arena in the United States Naturalization Petitions set on Findmypast; that collection begins too late for him. Nothing in the censuses of 1910, 1920 or 1930.
negative
GIOVANNI ARENA BAPTISED NO CHILD IN NEW YORK WITH CONCETTA RUSSO — Father GIOVANNI + mother CONCETTA, surname Arena: ZERO. There are EIGHT Arena baptisms with a father Giovanni — at Mount Carmel, Immaculate Conception, Mary Help of Christians and one at Our Lady of Loretto — and NOT ONE has a mother Concetta. The control ran first: father ROCCO + mother GENEROSA returns EXACTLY the four known children and nothing else.
negative
NO ARENA MARRIAGE AT OUR LADY OF LORETTO CAN BELONG TO THIS FAMILY — by arithmetic, not by guesswork — Seven Arena marriages at Our Lady of Loretto, 1897 to 1913: Rasolino 1897, Joannem 1899, Ignatium 1901, Aloissiam 1905, Joannem 1907, Paulum 1909, Calagerum 1913. The 1899 one was read and is a PALERMO family — father Thomae Arena, mother Angelicae Coglitore, bride of Polizzi Generosa.
negative
There is no New York Roman Catholic BURIALS collection — so the first Antonio's death cannot be tested here — «new york roman catholic parish burials» returns a 500 error: the set does not exist. «Congregational Records» resolves but is empty — zero Arena, and zero RUSSO, one of the commonest Italian surnames in New York. The Catholic Heritage Archive for New York is BAPTISMS AND MARRIAGES ONLY.
negative
The Victorian passenger lists hold nothing for this family — and the reason is what they are — «Victoria Coastal Passenger Lists 1852-1924» is DOMESTIC SHIPPING between Australian ports, and most of its rows carry NO GIVEN NAME AT ALL — the sample read is «Mazza, male, b. 1872, married, age 19, Strahan to Melbourne on the Bellinger, 14 December 1891». The identifiable pre-1925 Mazza in the outward lists are recorded as BRITISH. CONTROL: «Michele Mazza, 1925, Messina to Brisbane» is returned on the first try, so the collection does hold this family's one pre-war arrival.

The find: the family's New York parish

Two of Rocco Arena and Generosa Costa's New York children were baptised at Our Lady of Loretto — Sarafina Arena in 1898 and Clementina in 1901. Loretto was the Italian parish of Little Italy, on Elizabeth Street, a few minutes' walk from the Mulberry Street address the 1899 manifest gives for Rocco.

So the archive now knows not just the street but the church. The transcripts are locked behind a tier above the trial — and they would carry the godparents, who in an Italian parish are almost always kin. That is the single most valuable thing still sitting behind a paywall.

The negative worth most

The "Italy Births, Marriages and Deaths 1806–1900" sets do not cover Scilla. The test was clean: zero Polistena across all three, for a surname with six emigrants in the Ellis Island index alone. They are FamilySearch-derived and their Italian coverage runs to Sicily and the north, not Reggio Calabria. Anyone hoping to shortcut the Antenati work through a subscription site should know that before paying.

The same sets were tried for the archive's oldest Sicilian question — Rosario Mazza 's marriage to Leonarda Bonanno, about 1870. No Leonarda Bonanno marriage exists in the set, and no Mariano Mazza birth, though 110 Nicotra marriages show it does reach the Catania area. That question stays where it was.

Method, for whoever comes next. FindMyPast's search runs on a GraphQL endpoint — POST /titan/marshal/graphql, recordSearch(filters, order, pageNumber) under root { search }. Driving that directly turns a twelve-page click-through into one loop, and the RecordMetadataId filter restricts a search to a single record set. The whole sweep above cost a few minutes rather than an afternoon.

Coverage

What the archives hold, against what this one has listed

A negative is a claim that a record is not in a volume. It is worth nothing if the volume was never digitised, and worth less than nothing if the listing of volumes was incomplete and nobody said so. Twice this archive listed a comune once, never re-listed it, and planned searches around a fraction of what exists.

Piedimonte Etneo held 103 of its 457 registers until 20 September, when it was re-listed in full and reconciled group by group against the live index — it now holds 457. Briatico still holds 80 of 555. In both cases whole fondi were missing — the marriage processetti, the allegati, and at Piedimonte nine volumes of Riveli di anime e di terre from 1714 and 1747–1752 that nobody knew were there.

PlaceSeriesSpanThe holding Listed hereWhat that means
Scilla, Reggio Calabria Stato civile napoleonico 1809–1815 466 registers in three fondi 327 reachable — SCILLA'S REGISTERS BEGIN IN 1809, not 1816 -- a fact this archive got wrong for eight days and built a WALL on. Seventeen napoleonic volumes. Filippo Arena and Domenica Costa married in the fifth of them.
Scilla, Reggio Calabria Stato civile della restaurazione 1816–1865 180 volumes listed 180 reachable — The main run. Births, marriages, deaths and the MATRIMONI PROCESSETTI, twenty-two volumes 1810-1865 and none after.
Scilla, Reggio Calabria Stato civile italiano 1866–1943 130 volumes listed 130 reachable — Deaths 1911-1943 are also NAME-INDEXED on Antenati, with father, mother and spouse -- one query replaces a volume sweep.
Piedimonte Etneo, Catania Stato civile della restaurazione 1820–1865 223 registers 223 reachable — SICILY HAS NO NAPOLEONIC REGISTERS: the island stayed Bourbon and began only with the restoration, in 1820. Includes MATRIMONI PROCESSETTI 1841-1860, twenty volumes, never opened by this archive.
Piedimonte Etneo, Catania Stato civile italiano 1866–1893 225 registers 225 reachable — AND IT STOPS AT 1893. The Mazza-Nicotra marriage of about 1900 and Venera Cassaneti's death in 1925 are not here and never were.
Piedimonte Etneo, Catania Riveli di anime e di terre (Deputazione del Regno) 1714–1752 9 volumes 9 reachable — HOUSEHOLD TAX CENSUSES, a century before civil registration in Sicily -- which retires this archive's own rule that «before 1820 it is parish records or nothing». The 1747-1748 index is read: eighty households, no Mazza, two MAZZEO.
Piedimonte Etneo, Catania Civil Registration (Tribunale), via FamilySearch 1866–1929 not counted 0 another archive — THE ROUTE PAST 1893. The Tribunale's duplicate for the province of Catania runs thirty-six years further than the Archivio di Stato's filming, and this archive already cites the collection elsewhere. Not yet used for Piedimonte.
Briatico, Vibo Valentia Stato civile, all fondi 1810–1930 555 registers -- Catanzaro 440, Vibo Valentia 115 81 listing incomplete — THE WORST UNDER-LISTING IN THE ARCHIVE: eighty-one rows held against 555 available. A listing at s_size=500 returned 500 and showed NOTHING BETWEEN 1859 AND 1910 -- which would put Caterina Melluso's 1860 birth out of reach -- but fifty-five registers were not in the sample and that is not yet a finding.
Potenzoni (Briatico), Vibo Valentia Stato civile 1806 57 registers of its own 0 reachable — A FRAZIONE THAT REGISTERED SEPARATELY. Three honest negatives for Briatico births were collected from the wrong parish before this was found. Before recording a surname as absent from a comune, search its hamlets by name.
Zambrone, Vibo Valentia Stato civile napoleonico e restaurazione 1811–1844 11 volumes listed 11 a hole in the holding — THE DEATH RUN IS MISSING 1813-1821 and it is not an inference: the volume runs 1811, then 1812 twice -- once for Zambrone and once for SAN GIOVANNI, a hamlet with its own register -- then jumps to 1822. Holdings resume only in 1911. Three people this archive wants died inside that gap. THE ROUTE IS THE COMUNE'S OWN SECOND COPY, which is a letter and not a search.
Pizzo, Vibo Valentia Stato civile della restaurazione 1823 restaurazione only 0 a hole in the holding — STARTS IN 1823 -- tested on 1812, 1814, 1818 and 1822, all empty. That one fact killed three planned searches at once. And the 1837 CHOLERA register survives only as an index of acts 209-325, bound into the ordinary volume; the acts themselves are not digitised.
Messina Stato civile 1820–1865 366 volumes in 1820, rising to 510 by 1840 0 another archive — The province, not the city: MESSINA ITSELF appears in no Antenati year probed, though the series facet claims 1069 volumes. The 1908 earthquake is the obvious suspect and is NOT established. FamilySearch carries «Messina, Civil Registration (State Archive), 1820-1865» from a different filming -- try there before concluding a record is gone.
Mascali Stato civile 1820-1893 + Riveli 1616-1752 1616–1893 498 (Catania 452, Palermo 46) 498 reachable — LISTED WHOLE AND VERIFIED: 498 results, 498 distinct arks, and the facets total 498 exactly, so this is the holding and not a sample at the s_size ceiling. Opened 21 September off the 1872 marriage act, which put Giovanni Nicotra resident here. THE 1844 YEAR IS ABSENT from every restaurazione series -- 1843 then 1845. The 46 Palermo volumes are RIVELI, not civil registration: Tribunale del Real Patrimonio 1616-1678 and Deputazione del Regno 1714 and 1747-1752.
Giarre Stato civile 1820-1893 1820–1893 808 (all Catania) 808 reachable — THE SIXTH TOWN, opened 21 September off the words «nato a GIARRE» in Giovanni Nicotra's 1872 publication certificate. LISTED WHOLE AND VERIFIED, and it took TWO PAGE LOADS: s_size=500 returned 500 against a facet total of 808, and «&s_page=2» returned the remaining 308. 500 is a PAGE SIZE, not a ceiling. The largest comune in this archive -- 164 Nati, 147 Morti, 131 Matrimoni, 44 Matrimoni processetti -- and the ONLY ONE THAT HOLDS TWO COPIES OF EVERY YEAR, so a lost leaf in one may survive in the other. Unlike Mascali it has 1844.
Riposto Stato civile 1821-1860, 1866-1893 1821–1893 421 (all Catania) 421 reachable — THE SEVENTH TOWN, opened 21 September. LISTED WHOLE AND VERIFIED: 421 results, 421 distinct arks, facet total 421 -- complete in ONE page, under the s_size ceiling. IT REGISTERS SEPARATELY FROM GIARRE FROM 1821, right through the window in which Giovanni Nicotra was born, so «nato a Giarre» from a man of the lower town could be entered here. A GAP 1861-1865 in the middle, between the restaurazione run and the italiano one. AND UNLIKE GIARRE IT HAS ORDINARY NICOTRA HOUSEHOLDS: Giarre's only Nicotra in eleven years is a «Dottor Don», where Riposto 1847 alone holds two -- Giuseppe Nicotra x Angela Autieri and Salvadore Nicotra x Maria Barbagallo.
Holdings, volume by volume

Every register this archive has listed, and how much of it opens today

2601 volumes across 7 comuni, of which 325 can be opened right now — a container is what the image server needs, and buying one costs a page load against a limiter that blocks after about three. Every sweep on this site used to begin by re-deriving these numbers from five files and a memory. The bought column is counted from the banks, not asserted.

A blank in index means «not recorded», never «no index». Only Scilla's restauration bank carries that column at all, which is why the figure is small and why it is not a coverage claim.

Indexed by name

Where Antenati has done the indexing, the volume index does not matter

A comune whose acts are indexed by name answers a query in one request, with the father, the mother and the spouse — no volume, no Tavola, no sweep. Three of the eight comuni this family touches are indexed, and the act type differs between them, which is the trap: assuming Scilla's «deaths only» held everywhere would have been wrong at Pizzo.

  • Scilla, Reggio Calabria — Morti, 1911–1943
  • Pizzo, Vibo Valentia — Nati, 1868–1903
  • Catania — Morti, 1911 onward

Not indexed at all: Piedimonte Etneo, Catania, Briatico, Vibo Valentia, Zambrone, Vibo Valentia, San Costantino Calabro, Vibo Valentia, Potenzoni (Briatico), Vibo Valentia, Mascali, Catania, Giarre, Catania, Riposto, Catania. For these the annual Tavola is the instrument, and the work list records which years carry one.

Method, so it is not rediscovered. The Antenati register index takes ?localita=X&s_size=500 and returns the whole holding in one page load — but 500 is the ceiling, and above it the request times out. The true total is in the result facets, as Archivio di Stato di … (N), one line per holding archive. Compare the two before believing a listing is complete. A 500-of-555 listing reported as a gap is the same error as a half-read index section reported as a negative.

Everything read, and everything still to read

This page opened with the sentence “no Italian civil register has been opened for this family”, and that was the honest headline on the day the archive was built. It stopped being true a long time before it was taken down. What follows is every search, negatives included.

Outcome

235 searches · everything is listed; the filters only narrow it

35 of these 235 returned nothing, and that is the point of the page: a source that came back empty is the difference between «we have not looked» and «it is not there».

20 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Morti 1866–1943 doneEIGHTY-ONE volumes, and this archive did not know they existed. The annual index gives a patronymic — «di X» where the father lives, «fu X» where he does not — as well as the act number. The A block has been read for 1889–1896 and for 1897–1908.BOTH OF THEM FOUND AND THE ROW CLOSES. FRANCESCO ANTONIO ARENA, Morti 1901 atto 96, dead 29 August 1901; SERAFINA ARLOTTA, Morti 1908 atto 55, dead 24 April 1908 — both at VIA GROTTE NUMBER TWO. Twenty years of index reading, and the two acts are in the same house. Six unread Arena leads came out of the sweep and are listed on the sweep's own row.
Antenati — Scilla, Matrimoni pubblicazioni 1866–1913 outstandingFORTY-SIX volumes of banns, each with its own annual index — which is how the 1888 marriage was found when the marriage index itself was destroyed by water across exactly the A entries.Arena Rocco / Costa Generosa, under A in the 1888 banns index. Everything else in the series is unread.

13 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Nati 1854, act located by arithmetic doneThe 1854 Tavola carries no act numbers. Two acts to an opening, act = 66 + 2×(image−36), which puts late April at images 39–41.ACT 76. Giovanni Arena born 30 April 1854, declared by his grandfather GIOVANNI ARLOTTA, 75, marinaro, «figlio del fu DOMENICO». The Tavola said «Rosa Arlotta» and 20 April and was wrong twice. Giovanni Arena is Rocco's FULL BROTHER, and DOMENICO ARLOTTA is the deepest name on the Calabrian side.
13 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Matrimoni 1885 and 1888 doneThe corrected coverage note opened forty-eight volumes. 1885 for the Arena weddings; 1888 for Rocco Arena's own.THREE ACTS. 1885 atto 73 and atto 86: two of Rocco's brothers married seven weeks apart. 1888 atto 63: ROCCO ARENA'S OWN MARRIAGE ACT names his parents outright — Francesco Antonio Arena and Serafina Arlotta — and gives Generosa Costa's parents for the first time, Antonio Costa and ANNA ARLOTTA.
14 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Matrimoni pubblicazioni 1888 doneThe 1888 marriage index is destroyed by water across exactly the A entries. The BANNS are a separate series of 46 volumes with their own annual index.THE WAY ROUND A DESTROYED INDEX. Publication 63, 18 November 1888: Francesco Antonio Arena appears IN PERSON, alive, sixty-eight, a pescatore, a week short of sixty-nine. And the couple swore there was no impediment of kinship — so Rocco and Generosa were NOT first cousins.
14 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Nati 1855 doneThe year nobody had read. The archive had read 1851 (the tree's date), 1856 and 1859 (a declared age) and closed the search.MICHELE DONATO, BORN 3 OCTOBER 1855, act 163 — «DOMENICO DONATO, figlio di CANDELORO, trentotto, marinaro» and «NUNZIATA SOFI, trentasette». Three generations in one act, the ages exact at both ends, and the longest open question in the archive answered.
14 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Morti 1889–1896 (recorded at the time as 1889–1902) doneEighty-one volumes of deaths, 1866–1943, that this archive did not know existed. The annual index carries a PATRONYMIC as well as a name.CORRECTED 20 SEPTEMBER 2026, AND THE CORRECTION IS THE POINT. This row was written as «neither Francesco Antonio Arena nor Serafina Arlotta in thirteen years». FRANCESCO ANTONIO ARENA DIED IN 1901, act 96, inside the span this row claimed to have cleared. Either those later indexes were read and he was missed, or — far more likely, since the containers for 1897–1908 were still being bought three days later — the row counted years it had not opened. It does not matter which: A NEGATIVE IS ONLY WORTH THE PAGES ACTUALLY TURNED, and this one was written wider than its evidence. The years this archive can stand behind are 1889–1896. What the row did genuinely find stands: Fortunato Arena (ten days old, Via Grotte 70), Maria Donato (twenty-eight months, Via Grotte 21) and Orazio Arena — who turns out NOT to be the tree's Orazio.
14 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Matrimoni 1874–1885 and 1838–1847 doneMichele Donato's own marriage, eleven years; and his parents' marriage, six years.BOTH NEGATIVE, and both informative. Michele is neither born nor married at Scilla in the window — he came from elsewhere already married. And 1843 act 11 shows a DONATO ANTONIO «figlio di BRUNO»: there are at least TWO Donato lines in the town, which is why the Donato marriages of the 1870s are numerous and none is his.
14 Sep 2026FamilySearch — the New Orleans manifest of the Liguria, 1908, BOTH PAGES doneAmerican manifests from 1907 are two-page forms. This archive had only ever read page one.THE VERSO CARRIES COLUMN 29, PLACE OF BIRTH. Against line 15, Michele Donato: «R. Calabria / Scilla». Lines 9–11 of the same sheet say Tresilico, so the clerk was distinguishing. That sent the search back to the Scilla registers, where it belonged.
14 Sep 2026FindMyPast — passenger lists, marriages, censuses, parish index doneA one-week trial. Twenty-six outcomes recorded.Rocco Arena's four crossings named and shipped (Elysia 1892, Suevia 1894, Trave 1900, San Guglielmo 1913) and the 1894 no longer «probable». The 1913 manifest's RELATION columns name his wife and a nephew. Rocco Arlotta, the 1900 lodger, identified by his marriage and confirmed by his children's names. AND ONE RETRACTION: the Clementina Arena crossing this archive had held for months belongs to another family.
14 Sep 2026The archive's own negatives, audited doneNine negatives re-read — not repeated, but examined for what set each window.FIVE HAD ALREADY FAILED. Every failed window came from a declared age or an assumed eldest child. The rule: widen such a window by at least five years in both directions before a negative inside it means anything. Published at /audit.

15 Sep 2026Queensland BDM — beyond the six registrations doneThe Nudgee end of every one of the four quarters, searched on parents' names rather than the person's.Three mentions. The index is free and the technique that produced six registrations has not been run out. DONE 15 SEPTEMBER. All ten surnames swept by exact surname across births, marriages and deaths. The find was the CRUPI -- three brothers of Angela nobody here knew about. The rest is the shape of the index rather than of the family: POLISTENA and ANILE return NOTHING AT ALL and PROSTAMO returns ONE record, because births stop in 1926, marriages in 1951 and deaths in 1996 and these people arrived in 1949. ARENA returns seventy-five and not one is ours -- an Irish-Australian family entirely unconnected to Scilla.
Find a Grave — Nudgee Catholic cemetery outstandingPhotographs and inscriptions against the nineteen burials the Archdiocese register gave.Two mentions. An inscription often carries a birth date the register does not.
NARA New York — Rocco Arena's naturalisation outstandingHe is entered as a US CITIZEN on the manifests of both 1900 and 1913, so a declaration and petition exist from before March 1900.FindMyPast's petition set begins too late for him. This is a National Archives job, not a subscription one.
The Catholic Heritage Archive — Our Lady of Loretto transcripts outstandingEighty-four Arena baptisms are indexed and readable; the transcripts, which carry the MOTHER'S NAME and the GODPARENTS, are a higher subscription tier.Godparents in an emigrant parish are almost always kin. They would settle at a stroke whether the Giovanni Arena baptising a child in 1900 is Rocco's brother.
14 Sep 2026Geneanet — surname sweep donePROSTAMO, MELLUSO, ANILE, VENTRICE, ARLOTTA searched across all Geneanet record sets (trees, archives, transcriptions).No holding touches this family. PROSTAMO returns eight entries, every one a New Haven or Hamden, Connecticut vital record 1914-1955 — an American cluster of the surname, untested against Briatico and claimed as nothing. MELLUSO (506) is an Agrigento family. VENTRICE (730) is Ustica and French Algeria. ARLOTTA (881) is Buffalo, New York. ANILE (476) clusters at Pizzo, eleven km from Briatico, but no entry reaches our people.

Whether any of them is ours is untested and untestable without a subscription. Recorded as a door, not a finding.
14 Sep 2026Antenati — Briatico, Nati 1818 doneThe whole year read: 25 births, both pages of the Tavola.NEGATIVE, and complete. No child of any given name carries the surname Prostamo. 14 Sep 2026Antenati — Briatico, Nati 1819 doneThe whole year read: 34 births, all three Tavola pages.NEGATIVE, and complete. No Prostamo among the 34. 14 Sep 2026Antenati — the Potenzoni discovery doneSearched the register index on localita=Potenzoni after three straight years of Briatico births turned up no Prostamo at all.POTENZONI IS ITS OWN COMUNE ON ANTENATI, WITH 57 REGISTERS. It is a frazione of Briatico and it kept its own civil register from 1806. Everything read at Briatico so far has been the wrong parish if the family lived there. Births 1812-1837 are a single 364-image volume; marriages 1812-1858 a single 293-image volume; and the marriage allegati 1812-1850 hold the spouses' own birth certificates. This is the largest unopened door in the Calabrian half of the archive.
14 Sep 2026Antenati — Potenzoni, Nati 1812-1837 superseded364 images, an_ua37927182 / container 0AQJBAW. Domenico Prostamo, born about 1820, would fall in it.SUPERSEDED THE SAME DAY. This was opened on the reasoning that Domenico Prostamo, born about 1820, must be in the frazione if he was not in Briatico. His marriage act of 1849 then said he was NATO IN PIZZO and born in 1814. The volume is mapped and the mapping is kept, but this family is not in it.
14 Sep 2026Antenati — Potenzoni, Matrimoni 1812-1858 superseded293 images, an_ua37923835 / container 5K1ln7r. Domenico Prostamo married Giovanna Ventrice somewhere in this span.SUPERSEDED THE SAME DAY. Domenico Prostamo married Giovanna Ventrice at BRIATICO on 14 June 1849, act 1 — found in the Briatico register, not this one. The Potenzoni mapping is kept for whoever needs that comune.
14 Sep 2026Antenati — Potenzoni, Nati: the volume opened and mapped donean_ua37927182 / container 0AQJBAW, 364 images. Located the annual blocks: fourteen images to a year, two acts to an opening, each year closed by a Rubrica listing every birth in date order. 1818 index at image 66, 1819 at 80, 1820 at 104-106.PARTIAL. Potenzoni 1818 read in full (entries 12-21) and 1820 read in part (entries 7-16 of 23). No Prostamo in either. Two cautions recorded for whoever continues: the photographer left the 1819 Tavola and a title sheet lying on top of EVERY frame in this run, so the printed year visible at the head of an image is not the year of the page beneath it; and the volume is not purely Potenzoni — the register bound in at image 106 is headed «Comune di Briatico», for 1822, which suggests these are the second originals deposited with the tribunal and that Briatico years sit inside the Potenzoni unit.
14 Sep 2026Antenati — Briatico, Matrimoni 1840, 1841, 1842 doneRead by cropping the parroco's right-hand certificate box, which names both spouses, from every page and montaging them.NEGATIVE for Prostamo. 1840 has three marriages, 1841 about five, 1842 thirteen. Act 8 of 1842 was opened in full to test a suspected Prostamo in the margin: it is Giuseppe Artesi, 19, son of the late Nicola Artesi and Nicolina Corassi, marrying Alfonsina Petrarca, 15, born at Pizzinni, daughter of the late Francesco Antonio Petrarca and the late Rosa Arena. Not ours. The suspected name was a misreading of a 240-pixel thumbnail and was never claimed.
14 Sep 2026Trove — Prostamo, all collections doneKeyword PROSTAMO across newspapers, gazettes, books, archives and people.ELEVEN newspaper and gazette results, and the useful ones are all Commonwealth Gazette naturalisations at Brisbane: ASSUNTA MARIA PROSTAMO of 3 Frank Street, Graceville, naturalised 4 July 1966; DOMENICO FRANCESCO PROSTAMO of 38 Park Terrace, Sherwood, naturalised 1 October 1962; and ANGELA PROSTAMO, gazetted 16 November 1961. Not one Prostamo appears in a digitised Australian newspaper before 1961, and Domenica Prostamo's death on 3 May 1928 produced no notice that Trove holds. Weak evidence on its own — ordinary families rarely reach print — but it means the Brisbane newspapers will not date this family's arrival.

14 Sep 2026Antenati — Pizzo, the registers doneDomenico Prostamo was born at Pizzo about 1813; his mother Maria Fragalà was still living there in 1849; and the family tree puts his grandfather Pasquale's death at Pizzo on 4 January 1829.ANSWERED, AND IT IS A WALL. Pasquale Prostamo's death act of January 1829 was read. Domenico Prostamo's birth act of 1814 and Antonino Prostamo's marriage cannot be opened at all.

14 Sep 2026Antenati — Potenzoni, in hindsight doneOpened on the reasoning that three Prostamo-free years of Briatico births meant the family was in the frazione.THE REASONING WAS WRONG AND THE NEGATIVE WAS RIGHT. They were not at Potenzoni either; they were at Pizzo. Recorded because the mistake is instructive: an absence was read as evidence for a specific alternative rather than as evidence of absence, and the right move would have been to find a document that says where they were — which is what the marriage act eventually did. The Potenzoni mapping is kept; it is good work on a comune this family does not appear to belong to.
14 Sep 2026Antenati — Pizzo, Morti 1829 doneOpened the same afternoon the 1849 marriage act pointed at Pizzo. PASQUALE PROSTAMO, seventy-five, MARINARO, VEDOVO DI CATERINA MUSO, born at Pizzo, of the Castello quarter, died on 13 JANUARY 1829 — nine days after the date the tree gives, and at an age that puts his birth about 1753 rather than 1760. His own parents' lines in the act are blank. Acts 1 to 6 of the year were read to reach it and are other families.

The second generation, and why it may be out of reach not-yetPasquale Prostamo was born about 1753 and married before 1809.His death act leaves his parents blank, and civil registration in Calabria does not begin until 1809 — so his own birth and marriage are in PARISH books, which Antenati does not hold. The family tree's first two generations, 1697 and 1726, may not be testable online at all. Worth saying plainly rather than leaving them looking merely unresearched.

Antenati — Pizzo, the cholera register of 1837 not-yetAntonino Prostamo is act 327 in it. The chancery's 1849 extract quotes the entry; the register itself has not been opened.Worth opening for the act as it stands in the book, and for whoever else of this family the epidemic took. Act 327 by 31 December means the cholera killed on that scale in one town in one year.

14 Sep 2026Antenati — Pizzo, the 1837 cholera register: WHERE IT IS AND IS NOT doneOpened an_ua37926907 (Morti 1836-1840, container LevaEPW, 427 images) and read it end to end at thumbnail scale; also the Diversi 1827-1861 volume (an_ua37923734 / LmpVVqq, 121 images), the anno=1837 filter, and the full Pizzo register listing.THE CHOLERA REGISTER ITSELF IS NOT ON ANTENATI — BUT ITS INDEX IS, AND THE SHAPE OF THE VOLUME TELLS THE STORY. Pizzo's ORDINARY death register for 1837 runs to act 207 and stops on 16 JULY 1837 (images 89-194). From act 209 the town was entering its dead in a SECOND REGISTER. That register's pages are not digitised — but its TAVOLA IS, bound in at images 195-206, indexing acts 209 to 325 with names, ages, trades, quarters and relationships. Antonino Prostamo is act 327, just past the last indexed entry. So the certified extract of 20 May 1849 in the Briatico processetti is, as far as this archive can reach, THE ONLY READABLE TEXT OF ACT 327. Checked and excluded: the Diversi volume (it holds deaths outside the comune, not cholera); a separately catalogued 1837 Morti volume (the anno=1837 filter returns Nati and Matrimoni only); and a cholera section bound at the end of the Morti volume (the volume closes normally with 1840 and its Tavola).
14 Sep 2026Antenati — Pizzo 1837, the roll of the second register doneRead the whole surviving index, acts 209-325, by cropping the N. d'ordine and Nomi de' morti columns from all twenty-four pages and montaging them.NO PROSTAMO among the 117 indexed. The index stops at 325 and Antonino is 327. It does hold FOUR VENTRICE at Pizzo — Giovanni, 52, falegname, alla Piazza (act 251); Laura, 28, filatrice, al Castello (294); Benedetto, 26, filatore, all'Incoronata (295); and Diamante Aloi, 70, «vedova di Giuseppe Ventrice del Pizzo» (302). The bride's surname is at Pizzo as well as at Briatico and Zambrone. Recorded as a lead and nothing more: Ventrice is common on that coast and no document joins these to Giovanna.
The Pizzo cholera register of 1837, wherever it now is not-yetActs 209 to at least 327, July to December 1837. Not in Antenati's Pizzo holdings.It would be at the Archivio di Stato di Catanzaro or with the comune. It holds Antonino Prostamo's act as written, and the names of something over a hundred more people the epidemic took in one small town in six months.

14 Sep 2026Antenati — Zambrone, Matrimoni 1811-1858: THEIR MARRIAGE CANNOT BE IN IT donean_ua37928578 / container wWyoZJ2, 253 images, Zambrone's only pre-unification marriage volume. Opened, mapped by year, and sampled through the 1820s and 1830s.A STRUCTURAL NEGATIVE, AND A CERTAIN ONE. Domenico Ventrice was about fifty-eight when he died in January 1815, Anna Fama about fifty, and their son Giuseppe was born about 1809. Civil registration began in Calabria in 1809 and THIS VOLUME BEGINS IN 1811. A couple whose son was born in 1809 married before the register existed. Their marriage is in the PARISH books of Zambrone, which Antenati does not hold — the same wall this archive already met at Pizzo with Pasquale Prostamo. The volume can still be worth reading for their OTHER CHILDREN marrying, because every act and every annual index names both parents.
14 Sep 2026Antenati — Zambrone, the marriage indexes of 1822 and 1826 doneRead in full. Each year's Tavola carries a «Nomi e cognomi DEI GENITORI» column beside the couple, so one page settles a whole year.1822 holds six marriages and no Ventrice at all. 1826 holds six, and ONE Ventrice: SERAFINA VENTRICE married VINCENZO SORRENTINO on 9 August 1826, and she is «figlia di FRANCESCO e di CATERINA LA VESSA» — a DIFFERENT Ventrice household. So the surname is at Zambrone in the 1820s, but not our couple's children. Recorded because a second Ventrice family in the same small comune is exactly the trap this archive has been caught by before.
Antenati — Zambrone, the remaining marriage Tavole 1811-1858 not-yetAbout forty-eight annual indexes, one page each, every one naming the parents of both spouses. The volume is bound with each Tavola at the end of its year block; 1822's is at image 13, 1826's at 40, and the 1829-1831 blocks run to image 66.A bounded job that would find every child of DOMENICO VENTRICE and ANNA FAMA who married at Zambrone, and with them the couple's own names in black and white. Not done.

Act 12 — and a caution for the volume: THE TAVOLA'S «N. D'ORDINE» RUNS ONE BEHIND THE ACT NUMBERS. Its row 11 is act 12. Calibrate on a date before trusting the index's number. 14 Sep 2026Antenati — PIZZO BEGINS IN 1823, and three targets die with that fact doneTested the register index on localita=Pizzo for 1812, 1814, 1818 and 1822 — all empty — and on 1823, which returns four volumes. The full Pizzo listing carries only the RESTAURAZIONE fondo.PIZZO HAS NO NAPOLEONIC REGISTERS ON ANTENATI. Its civil records begin in 1823. So DOMENICO PROSTAMO'S BIRTH ACT (Pizzo Nati 1814, atto 285) cannot be opened, and neither can ANTONINO PROSTAMO AND MARIA FRAGALÀ'S MARRIAGE, which must fall before April 1814. Both survive only as the 1849 chancery copies in the Briatico marriage bundle. Together with Antonino's cholera act of 1837 and Domenico Ventrice's Zambrone act of 1815, that is FOUR of this family's deepest documents for which the bundle is the only readable text.

14 Sep 2026Queensland BDM — the complete MAZZA and MELLUSO surname sets doneRan the registrar's download endpoint with exactTermsFamilynameOnly=true on MAZZA and on MELLUSO, and tested the fathersname/mothersname fields properly.SEVENTEEN MAZZA REGISTRATIONS IN THE WHOLE INDEX — this archive held six. The eleven new ones are added, among them a MARIO MAZZA born 1900 whose parents are named at last (Rosario Mazza and Rosa Grasso), which turns the unjoined «Mario and Peppina» household into a named family; and an ANGELA MAZZA, born a CRUPI, who is a candidate for the unidentified Angela buried beside Rosario at Nudgee. MELLUSO returns exactly ONE registration in the entire index, so Gregorio Melluso has NO SIBLINGS OR CHILDREN recorded in Queensland. A METHOD CORRECTION WORTH KEEPING: the fathersname and mothersname fields RANK results, they do not FILTER them. Searching a parent's name alone returns an unfiltered hundred rows of strangers; the fields only bite when combined with a subject surname, and even then they only sort. Test them on a known case before believing a result.
14 Sep 2026Nudgee register against the Queensland index — the Angela beside Rosario doneMatched the Nudgee Catholic cemetery register on interment date against the Queensland death index, for the unnamed Angela in plot V-0116.IDENTIFIED. Nudgee interred ANGELA MAZZA on 13 January 1973 in V-0116; the Queensland index registers her death on 10 January 1973 and names her parents FRANCESCO CRUPI and MARIA VECCHIO. Plot V-0116 adjoins V-0117, where ROSARIO MAZZA (1899-1986) lies — and adjacent plots at Nudgee have meant a married pair every time this archive has tested it. So Rosario's wife has a maiden name at last. It does NOT settle the 1949 marriage of a Rosario Mazza to Annie Vergina Di Mauro: either that is another Rosario or this one married twice, and nothing found so far decides it.

14 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Matrimoni 1876 and 1877: the hunt for Michele Donato's marriage doneHis eldest son was born in 1878, so the marriage should fall about 1876-77. Both annual indexes read entire; the one Donato found was opened in full.STILL NOT FOUND, and one false lead closed. 1876 holds NO Donato on either side of any marriage. 1877 holds exactly one — DONATO CANDELORO, 31, marinaro, marrying ANNA MARIA BOVA on 16 September, act 43 — and the act names his father as the late ANTONINO Donato. Michele's father was DOMENICO Donato, figlio di Candeloro, so by the naming custom a Candeloro Donato marrying in 1877 looked exactly like his elder brother. It is a different household. CANDELORO IS A RECURRING DONATO GIVEN NAME AT SCILLA and cannot be used as a fingerprint. 1874, 1875 and 1878 are mapped with containers and unread.

The remaining years in the window are 1874 and 1880 onward; 1880's index is 98 images in and its M page was not reached.

14 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Matrimoni 1874 and 1875 doneBoth annual indexes opened, continuing the search for Michele Donato's marriage.1874 HOLDS A SECOND DONATO CANDELORO — act 62, marrying ROMANO DOMENICA — three years before the Candeloro Donato of act 43 of 1877. 15 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Matrimoni 1874, atto 62: Michele Donato's brother doneCalibrated the 1874 volume by reading the margin boxes: act 61 on image 32's left page, act 63 on image 33's left, so act 62 is the lower half of image 32's right page. The first document to show the Donato household whole, and BOTH PARENTS WERE ALIVE. Born about 1847, so he is the elder brother of Michele (born 3 October 1855) — which tightens the hunt for Domenico and Nunziata's own marriage to 1847 OR EARLIER. 1838-41, 1843 and 1847 are already negative; 1842, 1844, 1845 and 1846 are the years left. AND IT SETTLES THE WARNING RAISED YESTERDAY: the Candeloro Donato who married Anna Maria Bova in 1877 is the son of the late ANTONINO and Angela De Franco. Scilla held TWO Candeloro Donatos of marrying age at once.
15 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Matrimoni 1846, read entire doneCandeloro Donato was born about 1847, so his parents married in 1847 or earlier; 1838-41, 1843 and 1847 were already negative, leaving 1842, 1844, 1845 and 1846. All thirty-two acts read, one to an image: no Donato groom and no Sofi bride in any of them. Every bride is listed in scilla-reads so the year need never be opened again. 15 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Matrimoni 1845, read entire doneThirty-two acts, one to an image, read for both spouses and their parents.NEGATIVE FOR THE TARGET — no Domenico Donato, no Nunziata Sofi. Two of the four years in the window are now closed, 1845 and 1846; 1842 and 1844 remain. BUT THE YEAR HOLDS A DONATO WORTH KEEPING: act 31, August 1845, MICHELE DONATO, 31, BORN AT MESSINA, marinaro, living at Villa, «FIGLIO DI CANDELORO DONATO, marinaro, e di [—] PALADINO», marrying Angela de Amico. Recorded as a LEAD and explicitly not a join — the mother is a Paladino and this archive has no mother for Domenico; the man was born at Messina; and Scilla is proven to have held two unrelated Candeloro Donatos at once. It does establish that a Candeloro Donato was ALIVE in August 1845, the act saying «figlio di» and not «del fu».
15 Sep 2026The parent-link grades, and two that had to be taken back doneGraded the parent edges this archive has actually read, so the blood-relatives chart can draw a register-backed parent differently from one the tree asserts. data/parent-links.tsv now carries twenty-six rows.THIRTEEN EDGES GRADED READ AND TWO INDEX, out of about seven hundred and fifty. AND A WARNING BOUGHT THE HARD WAY: the mechanism can also ADD an edge the tree never had, and BOTH of the first two attempts to use it were WRONG. Piedimonte's Nati 1845 atto 64 names MARIANO MAZZA as Rosario's father — but the only Mariano Mazza in the export is a twentieth-century man, son of the Orazio born 1883, who cannot have fathered a boy in 1845. Nati 1843 atto 101 names ANNA CAVALLARO as Leonarda Bonanno's mother — but the only Anna Cavallaro in the export is the daughter of a Luciano Cavallaro BORN IN 1945. Both rows were written, both were caught by opening the record rather than trusting the slug, and both were removed. THE DOCUMENTED PARENTS ARE SIMPLY NOT IN THE EXPORT, and a name that matches is not a person that matches — the same lesson the two Candeloro Donatos of Scilla taught in the registers.
15 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Matrimoni 1844: the index exists after all doneOpened the tail of an_ua2002588 expecting no index, on yesterday's reading of 1845 and 1846.A CORRECTION OF MY OWN NOTE. 1844 DOES carry a «TAVOLA annuale alfabetica de' Matrimonj» at images 52-56, ruled for the spouses, their patria and professione, THEIR PARENTS, and the day of the church celebration — where 1845 and 1846 carry none at all. So the Bourbon run is not uniform and yesterday's flat claim was wrong. The table is marked INCHIOSTRO SBIADITO and GRINZE by the Archivio itself and needs the contrast stretched to read. Entries 23 to 32 are read and hold NO DONATO; the rest of the year is outstanding, and because the Tavola gives both parents of both spouses, finishing it would settle 1844 in a handful of images rather than thirty.
15 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Matrimoni 1844: DOMENICO DONATO AND NUNZIATA SOFI, FOUND doneThe 1844 annual Tavola is alphabetical by the groom's surname with the bride beneath him, so the D block was reachable directly once the table was found at images 48-56.ENTRY 17. «DONATO DOMENICO | Scilla | MARINARO | CANDELORO e PALADINO MARIA | Luglio» and beneath «SOFI NUNZIATA | id. | FILATRICE | PASQUALE e DE AMICO MARIA | 20». THE MARRIAGE THIS ARCHIVE HAS HUNTED THROUGH ELEVEN YEARS OF REGISTERS — 1838-41, 1843, 1845, 1846 and 1847 all read and all negative, and 1844 the year between them nobody had opened. Four new people: MARIA PALADINO, who is Candeloro Donato's wife and the first name this archive has had for her, and PASQUALE SOFI and MARIA DE AMICO. IT ALSO CONFIRMS YESTERDAY'S LEAD: the Michele Donato of act 31 of 1845, born at Messina, «figlio di Candeloro Donato e di [—] PALADINO», is Domenico's BROTHER. THE ACT ITSELF CANNOT BE READ — it is 23 or 24 of the year, the Archivio's cards say INCHIOSTRO SBIADITO and GRINZE, and the ink has gone. The Tavola is legible where the act is not.
15 Sep 2026Antenati — Archivio di Stato di Messina, whole province wallDoes civil registration at Messina reach back to Michele Donato's birth about 1814, and to his parents' marriage before it? Year-by-year probes of the province: 1815, 1819, 1820, 1821, 1825, 1840.NO, AND THE WALL IS EXACT. 1815 returns nothing. 1819 returns nothing. 1820 returns 366 volumes across seventeen comuni, 1821 more, 1840 five hundred and ten. CIVIL REGISTRATION IN THE PROVINCE OF MESSINA BEGINS IN 1820 — six years after Michele Donato was born there, and later still than his parents' marriage. Sicily had no Napoleonic period, so it has no «Stato civile napoleonico»: the series starts with the restoration, and starts late. So CANDELORO DONATO IS WHERE THE SCILLA LINE STOPS in the civil registers, exactly as Pasquale Prostamo is where the Calabrian one does. Anything earlier is a PARISH record, in the Archdiocese of Messina, and not on Antenati. AND A SECOND WALL BEHIND THE FIRST: the comune of MESSINA ITSELF appears in none of the years read — not 1820, not 1840 — although the archive's series list claims 1069 volumes for it. Messina city was destroyed by the earthquake of 28 December 1908, which killed some eighty thousand people and took much of its civil archive with it. That is to be confirmed rather than assumed, but it means the city is the worst-served comune in its own province and the Donato birth may be unreachable by any route.
15 Sep 2026Brisbane City Council cemeteries — the whole register, by API doneAll nine Council cemeteries and crematoria — Mount Gravatt, Toowong, Pinnaroo Lawn, Balmoral, Hemmant, Lutwyche, South Brisbane and the rest, 360,000 records. Searched: Mazza, Prostamo, Polistena, Arena, Donato, Melluso, Nicotra, Crupi, Cavallaro.THE ENDPOINT, WHICH IS THE USEFUL PART: Council's search moved to «EverAfter», and behind it is clean JSON needing no login — GET https://brisbane.discovereverafter.com/api/deceased-search?name=SURNAME&country=&facility_id=&order_by=relevance&sort=desc . It returns forename, middlename, surname, date of birth, date of death, age, date of burial or cremation, and the cemetery. The match is FUZZY -- a search for Mazza returns Mazzaracca, Mazzaglia and Mazzaroli -- so filter on the surname yourself. FOUR NAMES RETURN NOTHING AT ALL across every Council cemetery: POLISTENA, MELLUSO, NICOTRA and CRUPI. That is not a failure, it is the shape of the family: they were Catholic and they are at NUDGEE, which the Archdiocese runs and Council does not list. WHAT IT CORRECTED: Domenico Francesco Prostamo's birth, recorded here as 1931, is 18 OCTOBER 1930 in Council's own record -- which agrees with the family tree and makes him ninety-four when he died in January 2025, not ninety-three. WHAT IT ADDED, AS LEADS AND NOT JOINS: MARIO MAZZA (1921-2015) and MARIA MAZZA (1931-2014) share plot Mon 3H 104 at Mount Gravatt, which in these registers means a married pair -- and neither is in Michael's export. AND AT PINNAROO LAWN, A DONATO FAMILY CARRYING THIS LINE'S TWO RAREST NAMES: «Gregory CANDELORO Donato» (1941-2025), «Donato CENDELORD» (d. 1968 at 52), «Donato ANNUNZIATO» (d. 1954 at 66), with a Rocco and a Concetta beside them. Candeloro is the name of the Scilla grandfather born about 1783 and of his grandson born 1847; Annunziata is Nunziata Sofi's. THE EXPORT CONTAINS NO CANDELORO AT ALL. This is exactly the shape of a join this archive has got wrong four times, so it is written down as a lead and nothing more. The Queensland death index names parents and would settle both.
15 Sep 2026Antenati — the Briatico processetti, mapped as a series doneWhich years exist, which comuni they cover, and how they are arranged. Probed 1820, 1822, 1824, 1826, 1828, 1830, 1840, 1845, 1850.THEY EXIST FOR MOST YEARS, AND THE SERIES IS NOT ONE COMUNE. Briatico returns TWO OR THREE separate processetti arks for the same year, and they are DIFFERENT COMUNI in the same circondario -- 1826 gives Briatico (an_ua37920142, 59 images) and SCICONI (an_ua37929531, 52 images); 1828 gives three plus a CONIDONI bundle covering 1828-1851 (an_ua37920221, 318 images). Read the manifest label before assuming which town a bundle belongs to. Years found: 1824 (an_ua37920082, 107 images), 1826, 1828, 1830, 1840, 1845, 1850. NOT 1820 and NOT 1822. AND THEY ARE ARRANGED DIFFERENTLY FROM SCILLA'S, which matters for what a search costs. Scilla's bundles are PER-MARRIAGE FASCICOLI, each opening with a cover sheet that names both spouses and indexes its own contents -- so the right file is found by reading covers. BRIATICO'S 1826 BUNDLE HAS NO COVER SHEETS AT ALL: it is a continuous run of loose notificazioni and certificates, «PARTE II», with the spouses' names in the body of each. SO THE ROUTE IN IS THE MARRIAGE REGISTER FIRST. Get the act number from the Briatico Matrimoni volume, then find the corresponding papers. Reading the bundle cold means reading every page. The target that prompted this: GIUSEPPE VENTRICE x CATERINA CICHELLO, married before mid-1827 -- their daughter Giovanna was born 23 June 1828. Their bundle would carry Giuseppe's own birth act (about 1795), Caterina Cichello's, HER PARENTS, and very possibly the death of PAOLO VENTRICE, which would reach a generation this archive has only as a name. NOT YET FOUND.
15 Sep 2026FamilySearch — UNBLOCKED after five days, and what it actually holds doneRetried after the site-wide Error 15 that named the proxy IP and blocked it from the day this archive was built. Searched Prostamo, Francesco Prostamo and Rosario Mazza.IT WORKS, AND IT NEEDS NO LOGIN. The index returns names, exact dates, places AND BOTH PARENTS on the results page itself. The surname match is fuzzy -- «Prostamo» returns Prada, Pretta, Pretto and Preiti -- so add `q.surname.exact=on`, which cuts 1,335 results to 26. THE COLLECTION THAT MATTERS FOR THE CALABRIAN LINE: «Italy, Vibo Valentia, Vibo Valentia, Civil Registration (TRIBUNALE), 1861-1910» -- the COURT copy, which runs to 1910 where Antenati's comune copies stop at 1900. BUT NOT FOR BRIATICO. Twenty-six exact Prostamo births 1896-1912 are indexed and every one of them is MILETO, COMPARNI, SAN GIOVANNI or PIZZO. Not one is Briatico. FRANCESSCO PROSTAMO, BORN 16 NOVEMBER 1900 AT BRIATICO, IS NOT IN THE INDEX -- so Briatico's 1900-1910 volumes are either unindexed browse-only images here or absent, and that hope, which has sat in the queue since 10 September, is not a quick one after all. PIZZO IS INDEXED, which is worth knowing given the Prostamo are a Pizzo family: Giorgio Prostamo d. 1903 and Anna Prostamo d. 1899, both of Antonino/Antonio and Angela Conde. Too late for the spine but the same town. AND ONE THING THAT REFINES TODAY'S MESSINA FINDING. FamilySearch carries «Italy, MESSINA, Civil Registration (STATE ARCHIVE), 1820-1865». That CONFIRMS INDEPENDENTLY that Sicilian registration begins in 1820 -- and it softens the second wall: Messina's records for 1820-65 SURVIVE and are filmed, even though the comune of Messina appeared in no Antenati year probed. The earthquake of 1908 did not take everything. It does NOT move the first wall: Michele Donato was born about 1814, still six years early.
15 Sep 2026Antenati — Briatico marriages 1822 to 1827, read complete doneGIUSEPPE VENTRICE x CATERINA CICHELLO, whose daughter Giovanna was born 23 June 1828, so the marriage must precede it. Six whole years read from the annual Tavole: 1822, 1823, 1824, 1825, 1826, 1827.NEGATIVE, AND FIRM. TWENTY-FOUR MARRIAGES IN SIX YEARS AND NOT ONE VENTRICE. 1822 had five. 1823 had five, 1824 four, 1825 four or five, 1826 five, AND 1827 HAD NONE AT ALL -- its Tavola is ruled and completely empty. WHERE TO LOOK NEXT, and the negative points at it: Giuseppe Ventrice DIED at Briatico in 1831, but his father DOMENICO VENTRICE died at ZAMBRONE in 1815. The family was a Zambrone family that moved. The marriage is most likely at ZAMBRONE, or earlier than 1823 at Briatico, or Giovanna was not the first child. AND A METHOD FINDING WORTH MORE THAN THE NEGATIVE. BRIATICO'S MARRIAGE VOLUMES ARE TINY AND EVERY ONE CARRIES AN ANNUAL TAVOLA THAT NAMES BOTH SETS OF PARENTS. The volumes run six to thirteen images; the Tavola is the LAST image or the one before it, ruled for «Nomi e cognomi dei Sposi | PATRIA | NOMI E COGNOMI DE' GENITORI | Giorno della celebrazione». So a whole year of Briatico marriages, with four parents for every couple, costs ONE IMAGE. The series runs 1816 to 1858 and the arks are sequential -- ark(Y) = an_ua87654 + (Y - 1840), which is CONFIRMED BACKWARDS as well: 1824 is an_ua87638, 1827 is an_ua87641. The entire Briatico marriage series could be swept for about forty images. Nothing else in this archive is that cheap. AND THE TAVOLA CONVENTION STARTS SOMEWHERE AROUND 1822: the volumes for 1816 and 1817 end on ACTS, not an index, so those years must be read act by act. The remaining window for this marriage is therefore 1816-1821 at Briatico -- about seventy images, all downloadable, none indexed -- or Zambrone. Containers found: 1816 5VKDrmE, 1817 5dZbKXn, 1818 wRGR73a, 1819 Lydrvn1, 1820 wlGnjVD, 1821 w68pynN, 1822 wbrz4Vg, 1823 w99lEay, 1824 wXrR28W, 1825 LNjzb4J, 1826 0JbOyEd, 1827 0A4Xgj9.
15 Sep 2026Antenati — the Zambrone marriage book, first fifty images (1811-1830) partGIUSEPPE VENTRICE x CATERINA CICHELLO, after Briatico 1822-27 came back negative. Zambrone Matrimoni 1811-1858, an_ua37928578, container wWyoZJ2, 253 images. Annual Tavole read complete for 1822, 1824 and 1826; the acts of the other years not yet read.NOT FOUND YET, BUT THE FAMILY IS THERE, WHICH IS WHAT THE BRIATICO NEGATIVE PREDICTED. TAVOLE READ COMPLETE: 1822 (five marriages -- Scarpato x Sambiase, Coglia x Adamo, Pungitori x Pietrapaolo, Trillo x Colica, Trillo x Ferraro) and 1824 (three -- Piatto x Viggo, Trillo x Pellegrino, Sorrentino x Colica). NO VENTRICE IN EITHER. AND A VENTRICE AT LAST, in the Tavola at image 40: «VINCENZO SORRENTINO x SERAFINA VENTRICE», celebrated a 9 Agosto. HER FATHER READS AS FRANCESCO -- so a different branch from Domenico Ventrice's, and not the couple wanted. THE YEAR IS NOW SETTLED: 1826. Each year in this volume ends on UNUSED BLANK PRINTED FORMS before its Tavola -- image 34 is a blank 1825 form, image 39 a blank 1826 form -- and the Tavola at image 40 follows the 1826 blanks. So SORRENTINO x SERAFINA VENTRICE WERE MARRIED AT ZAMBRONE ON 9 AUGUST 1826, and 1826's three marriages hold no Giuseppe Ventrice. ONE CAUTION REMAINS: the parents' column is cramped and faded, so FRANCESCO -- which would make Serafina a different branch from Domenico Ventrice's -- is a reading and not a certainty. An act of 23 January 1830 at image 50 also names a Vincenzo Sorrentino «sposo di Serafina Ventrice», which may be the same couple's civil promise or may be a second entry -- unresolved. STRUCTURE, which makes the rest cheap: each year is a printed title page, then acts one to an opening, then an annual TAVOLA naming BOTH SETS OF PARENTS. Located so far -- image 13 Tavola 1822, image 18 a BLANK 1823 form, image 27 Tavola 1824, image 28 title 1825, image 35 title 1826, image 40 a Tavola, image 48 a Tavola, image 49 title 1830. SO FOUR YEARS ARE NOW CLEAR -- 1822, 1824, 1826 at Zambrone, on top of Briatico 1822-1827 -- AND THE WINDOW IS 1811-1821, 1823, 1825 AND 1827. Giuseppe Ventrice was born about 1795, so 1818-1825 is where to look first. All fifty images are downloaded.
15 Sep 2026Antenati — Zambrone Matrimoni 1824, atto 2. FOUND. doneGIUSEPPE VENTRICE x CATERINA CICHELLO, hunted across Briatico 1822-1827 and Zambrone 1822-1826 before this.FOUND, ON 2 MARCH 1824 AT ZAMBRONE, and it was three images from where the sweep had already reached. The reason it took so long is worth recording: THE TAVOLE IN THIS VOLUME LEAVE THEIR YEAR BLANK. The printed header reads «nell'anno ___» and nobody filled it in, so the year has to be deduced from position -- and I deduced it wrong, calling the Tavola at image 27 «1824» when its three couples are the acts of 1823. Reading the ACTS, which carry «L'Anno mille ottocentoventiquattro» in print, settled it in one look. RULE: IN THIS SERIES, TRUST THE ACTS FOR THE YEAR AND THE TAVOLA ONLY FOR THE NAMES. What the act gives is on the plate and in the corrections: Caterina Cichello's parents FRANCESCO CICHELLO and VITTORIA MOLLO, a couple entirely new to this archive; Giuseppe's birthplace S. COSTANTINO; all four parents already dead by 1824; and a direct contradiction of the 1849 chancery copy over whether Domenico Ventrice's wife was ANNA FAMA or ANNA MAROPOLI.
15 Sep 2026Antenati — Zambrone Morti 1811-1839, opened. THE GAP IS REAL AND NOW EXACT. doneWhether Zambrone's deaths really are missing 1813-1821 — a wall this archive has published for days without ever opening the volume. Zambrone Morti 1811-1839, an_ua37932358, container w9dp62r, 234 images.THE WALL IS REAL, AND IT IS NOT AN INFERENCE ANY MORE. The volume runs 1811 (images 1-24), then 1812, which ENDS WITH ITS OWN DEATH TAVOLA at images 37-38, then a printed title page at image 39 — AND THEN IMAGE 40 IS 1822. Acts 1, 3, 5, 7, 9 and 11 of 1822 run from image 40 to image 45. SO THE REGISTER JUMPS FROM THE END OF 1812 STRAIGHT TO 1822 AND THE YEARS 1813 TO 1821 ARE PHYSICALLY ABSENT. Nine years, and this archive had them right. WHAT THAT COSTS: DOMENICO VENTRICE DIED 13 JANUARY 1815, squarely inside the gap. His original death act is NOT in this volume, so the ANNA FAMA against ANNA MAROPOLI question CANNOT be settled from Zambrone's own register. BUT THE SURNAME IS CORROBORATED ANYWAY, and from an unexpected direction: the 1822 death Tavola names a «fu Franc.co e Dom.ca MAROPOLI» as the parents of a Francesca Pungitori. MAROPOLI IS A REAL ZAMBRONE SURNAME OF EXACTLY THIS PERIOD, which is what a reading of «della fu Anna Maropoli» needed and did not have. It does not prove the 1849 copy wrong; it removes the objection that the name looked invented. AND THE VOLUME IS CHEAP TO SWEEP: EVERY YEAR ENDS WITH AN ANNUAL TAVOLA ruled for «Nomi e Cognomi dei Morti | Patria | Professione | NOMI E COGNOMI DE' GENITORI | Giorno della Morte». 1812's is at image 37, 1822's at image 51. One image a year, with the parents of every person who died. 1822 READ COMPLETE: seven deaths, and NEITHER Francesco Cichello NOR Vittoria Mollo is among them — though a MARIA Mollo died on 27 May 1822.
15 Sep 2026Antenati — Zambrone death Tavole 1822, 1823 and 1824, read complete doneFRANCESCO CICHELLO and VITTORIA MOLLO, both already dead when their daughter married on 2 March 1824 — so their deaths fall somewhere before that. And ANNA MAROPOLI, Giuseppe Ventrice's mother, also «la fu» by 1824. Zambrone Morti 1811-1839: Tavola 1822 at image 51, 1823 at image 65, 1824 at image 78.NEGATIVE FOR ALL THREE, AND THE NEGATIVE POINTS SOMEWHERE USEFUL. The three annual death Tavole name everyone who died at Zambrone in 1822, 1823 and 1824, WITH THEIR PARENTS -- and NEITHER FRANCESCO CICHELLO NOR VITTORIA MOLLO IS AMONG THEM. Nor is Anna Maropoli. SO ALL THREE ALMOST CERTAINLY DIED IN THE NINE MISSING YEARS, 1813-1821 -- the same gap that hides Domenico Ventrice's act of January 1815. Four people this archive wants, behind one gap. That makes the SECOND COPY of the register (work list row 59) the single most valuable lead left on the Calabrian side. WHAT THE SWEEP DID SETTLE: MAROPOLI IS UNMISTAKABLY A ZAMBRONE SURNAME. It appears TWICE more in these three years -- «fu Franc.co e Dom.ca MAROPOLI» as parents in the 1822 Tavola, and GIUSEPPE MAROPOLI himself, an infant, dead 23 March 1824, son of Antonio and Marianna Colia. Two independent attestations inside two years. The reading «della fu Anna Maropoli» on the 1824 marriage act no longer needs defending as a reading; what remains open is only which of two documents is right. AND A PLACE WORTH NOTING: the 1824 Tavola's first entry is FRANCESCA CAPIGRELLI OF S. COSTANTINO -- the same S. Costantino where Giuseppe Ventrice was born. Zambrone's register carries S. Costantino people, so the two towns shared a population and the Ventrice move was a short one. MOLLO AND CICHELLO ARE BOTH LOCAL: a Maria Mollo died in 1822, a Marianna Mollo and an Anna Cichello in 1823, a Domenica Mollo appears as a parent in 1824. Neither surname is rare here, which is worth remembering before any join is made on one.
15 Sep 2026FamilySearch catalogue — attempted, and it was down partThe second copy of Zambrone's civil registers for 1813-1821, the nine years missing from the Antenati volume.NOT RULED OUT -- NOT CHECKED. FamilySearch's catalogue returned «Something Went Wrong» for every query on 15 September. Its JSON endpoint at /service/search/catalog/search is alive and validates query terms -- `q.title`, `q.subject` and `q.place` are accepted where `q.text`, `q.keyword` and `q.anyPlace` are rejected -- but `q.place=Zambrone` IGNORES the value and returns two million hits, so it is useless without a place ID. RETRY LATER; this is a service outage and not a negative. WHAT THE ATTEMPT DID PRODUCE, by making me check the premise instead: THE VOLUME ANTENATI HOLDS IS THE COURT'S COPY, NOT THE COMUNE'S. Its manifest says «Archivio di Stato di Catanzaro > Stato civile napoleonico > Zambrone > 1811-1839», and under the Napoleonic and Bourbon codes every act was written TWICE -- one register for the comune, one for the tribunale, the tribunale's going to the Archivio di Stato. SO THE NINE YEARS ARE MISSING FROM ONE OF TWO COPIES, and the other may still be at the COMUNE DI ZAMBRONE. That is supported by the act having existed in 1849, when the chancery certified a copy of Domenico Ventrice's 1815 death for a marriage at Briatico -- something was still being copied from, thirty-four years on. It makes the next step a LETTER to the comune rather than another search, and it is the only route to four people: Domenico Ventrice, Anna Maropoli, Francesco Cichello and Vittoria Mollo.
15 Sep 2026Queensland BDM — swept by surname for Donato, Mazza and Crupi partTesting the two Brisbane leads found in the council cemeteries — the Pinnaroo Donato carrying CANDELORO, and Mario and Maria Mazza of Mount Gravatt — and sweeping the surname sets properly rather than by targeted lookup. The endpoint 403s to curl and must be fetched from the site's own origin in the browser.THREE RESULTS, AND ONE OF THEM IS A FAMILY NOBODY HERE KNEW ABOUT. CRUPI -- ANGELA CRUPI HAD AT LEAST THREE BROTHERS IN QUEENSLAND. Her own death registration of 1973 names FRANCESCO CRUPI and MARIA VECCHIO. So do THREE MORE: PIETRO CRUPI (d. 18 July 1961), ANTONINO CRUPI (d. 15 April 1982) and MARIO CRUPI (d. 17 September 1989). Four siblings, one parent-pair, and this archive knew one of them. Two of the brothers also have Queensland marriages in 1940. DONATO -- THE PINNAROO FAMILY IS FULLY DOCUMENTED AND IS NOT DEMONSTRABLY OURS. ANNUNZIATO DONATO and CONCETTA BRIGANTI had five children registered in Queensland: Rocco 1912, Concetto 1913, Peppino 1914, CANDELORO 1915 and Nunzio 1917, and the deaths of four of them repeat the same parents. AND ANNUNZIATO'S OWN DEATH OF 28 FEBRUARY 1954 NAMES HIS PARENTS: ROCCO DONATO and CONCETTA ROMANO. NOT Michele Donato and Maria Sofi. So the Candeloro at Pinnaroo is a grandson of a ROCCO Donato, not of this archive's line. THE NAMES REMAIN SUGGESTIVE AND THAT IS ALL THEY ARE: Candeloro, Annunziato, Rocco, Nunzio and Concetto are precisely the Scilla Donato naming pool, and CONCETTA ROMANO carries a surname this archive has already met at Scilla in Giovanni Polistena's mother. A cousin line is plausible. A DOCUMENTED LINK IS ABSENT, and this is exactly the join this archive has got wrong four times. MAZZA -- THE MOUNT GRAVATT PAIR CANNOT BE TESTED HERE AT ALL. Queensland's public death index stops at 1996 and MARIO MAZZA DIED IN 2015, MARIA MAZZA IN 2014. The Mario Mazza who appears in the index died in 1986 and is a different man, son of Rosario Mazza and Rosa Grasso. The lead is neither confirmed nor refuted; it is out of the index's reach. The sweep also re-confirmed five registrations this archive already held -- Maria Nicotra 1963, Mary Rosa Mazza 1968, Rosario Mazza 1986, Michele Mazza 1926 and Angela Crupi 1973 -- with the same parents each time.
15 Sep 2026Queensland BDM — the rest of the surnames swept doneProstamo, Polistena, Melluso, Nicotra, Anile, Barbuto and Arena, by exact surname, births marriages and deaths.MOSTLY EMPTY, AND THE EMPTINESS IS INFORMATIVE. POLISTENA RETURNS NOTHING AT ALL, and so does ANILE. That is not a gap in the family, it is the shape of the index: births stop in 1926, marriages in 1951, deaths in 1996, and the Polistena reached Brisbane in 1949. They arrived too late to be born or married into it and have mostly died too late to be in it. PROSTAMO RETURNS EXACTLY ONE RECORD in the entire Queensland index -- Maria Assunta, 1991 -- for a family with a dozen people in Brisbane. Same reason. ARENA RETURNS SEVENTY-FIVE AND NONE OF THEM IS OURS: Byrne, Kerby, Pollard, Timothy Leo, Cornelius Joseph, Alphonso James -- an IRISH-AUSTRALIAN Arena family entirely unrelated to Scilla. A surname sweep without a parent check would have produced a dozen false joins here. NICOTRA returns eighteen, of which ONE is ours -- Maria Nicotra, 1963, already held. The rest are a Catania Nicotra cluster around Maccarone, Patane and Scalia. BARBUTO returns two, neither ours. AND A CORRECTION TO MY OWN EXPECTATION: the Melluso and Prostamo registrations I treated as finds were ALREADY IN data/qld-bdm.tsv, recorded by an earlier session with better analysis than mine -- including that LIMARDO is a Potenzoni surname from the 1818 birth index. What was actually missing was that GENNARO MELLUSO, MARIA LIMARDO and CARMELA MARIA BORELLO had never become PEOPLE. They are now. The lesson is about this archive, not about Queensland: facts recorded in one file do not reach the reader until they are surfaced in another.
15 Sep 2026FamilySearch — Australian records for Prostamo partLooking for AUSTRALIAN ELECTORAL ROLLS, promoted to the primary instrument after Queensland BDM proved unable to see a family that arrived in 1949.NO ELECTORAL ROLLS THERE. FamilySearch's Australian holdings for this surname are Petty Sessions registers, Find a Grave and nothing else -- the rolls are on Ancestry, which is paid, and at the National Library. So row 62 still needs a source and this was not it. TWO THINGS THE SEARCH DID GIVE. FIRST, A PLACE AND A DATE FOR ROCCO PROSTAMO. «Australia, Victoria, Petty Sessions Registers, 1858-1985» indexes a PROSTAMO at NORTHCOTE between November 1956 and June 1957, and at HEIDELBERG between December 1957 and June 1958 -- both Melbourne. The index garbles the given name as «Ricco» and «Rono», which is this data's usual treatment of ROCCO. It fits: Rocco Prostamo, born 28 January 1925, is buried at NORTHERN MEMORIAL PARK, GLENROY, in the same city. WHAT THE ENTRIES CONCERN IS NOT STATED IN THE INDEX AND HAS NOT BEEN READ -- petty sessions covered licences, debts, permits and minor matters alike -- so this is recorded as a PLACE AND A DATE and nothing more. It should not be characterised further without reading the register itself. SECOND, A CAUTION ABOUT FIND A GRAVE. It indexes FRANCESCA PROSTAMO, born 18 September 1933 and died 11 February 2018, at TWO DIFFERENT CEMETERIES ON TWO CONTINENTS -- «Northern Memorial Park, Glenroy, Victoria» and «Northern Cemetery, Barnesville, Belmont County, OHIO». Same dates, same name, one person. The two cemeteries share the word NORTHERN and that is almost certainly the whole explanation. This archive uses the Victorian one, because she landed at Fremantle in 1957 and Rocco is buried in the same Melbourne ground. A Find a Grave hit is not a burial until the cemetery agrees.
15 Sep 2026Antenati — San Costantino Calabro, mapped for the first time partThe town named in Giuseppe Ventrice's marriage act of 1824 as his birthplace — «nato in S. Costantino» — and never once queried by this archive.IT EXISTS, IT IS LARGE, AND IT REACHES BACK FURTHER THAN ZAMBRONE'S SURVIVING DEATHS. 205 volumes, 1810 to 1912, including NATI 1812-1824 and 1813-1860, MORTI 1813-1827, 1828-1837 and 1838-1849, MATRIMONI 1813-1832, and FORTY-EIGHT volumes of MATRIMONI PROCESSETTI. WHY THIS MATTERS MORE THAN A NEW TOWN USUALLY WOULD: ZAMBRONE'S DEATH REGISTER IS MISSING 1813-1821, and four people this archive wants died inside that gap. SAN COSTANTINO'S MORTI 1813-1827 COVERS EXACTLY THOSE YEARS. If ANNA MAROPOLI -- Giuseppe Ventrice's mother, «la fu» by March 1824 -- died in her husband's own town rather than at Zambrone, HER ACT SURVIVES. The gap may be walkable round rather than through. GIUSEPPE'S OWN BIRTH IS STILL OUT OF REACH: he was about 1790 and the registers begin in 1810. AND THE USUAL TRAP, MET AGAIN: the volume labelled «San Costantino 1813-1827» (an_ua37932372, container wRDnEp4, 325 images) IS NOT ONE COMUNE. Image 12 is San Costantino; images 24 and 40 are FRANCICA. Read the comune line on every act, not the series label. NOT YET SWEPT -- 325 mixed images, and the year structure has not been mapped.
15 Sep 2026Antenati — Pizzo, the whole series listed partEverything Antenati holds for Pizzo, to see what is reachable above and around the Prostamo wall.751 VOLUMES, AND THE WALL IS CONFIRMED FROM THE SERIES ITSELF: the earliest is 1823, exactly as this archive has said. MARIA FRAGALA was alive at Pizzo in June 1849 and is the one person on this line whose death falls INSIDE the surviving run. 15 Sep 2026Brisbane cemeteries — the three Crupi brothers, both registers donePIETRO (d. 1961), ANTONINO (d. 1982) and MARIO CRUPI (d. 1989), found today in the Queensland death index as brothers of Angela Crupi. Searched Brisbane City Council's nine cemeteries and the Catholic Archdiocese's nine, including wildcard variants Crup%, Crupi% and Cruppi.NOT IN EITHER REGISTER. Council returns ZERO Crupi across all nine of its cemeteries and crematoria. The Catholic register returns EXACTLY ONE, and it is none of them: VENERO CRUPI, born 14 October 1935, died 4 December 2023, interred at NUDGEE on 13 December 2023, plot LA-3299. SO THE THREE BROTHERS ARE BURIED SOMEWHERE NEITHER REGISTER COVERS -- outside Brisbane, or cremated privately, or in a ground held by neither the council nor the Archdiocese. Their death registrations carry no C country code, so they died in the Brisbane district, which makes the absence odder rather than less. VENERO CRUPI IS A LEAD AND IS LEFT AS ONE. Venero is a Catania name and Angela Crupi was born at Catania in 1900, so he fits the family's origin exactly -- and fitting an origin is not a descent. He died in December 2023, well within living memory, and the people who could confirm him are alive. This is a question for Michael, not for an index.

ROSARIO MAZZA, shopkeeper, and ANGELA MAZZA, home duties, at 225 LEICHHARDT STREET, Brisbane city, 1959, on consecutive roll numbers 1844 and 1845, which is one household; Rosario is Sebastiano's brother and Angela is very probably Angela Cupo.
