20 Sep 2026NAA — the Crupi internment dossiers, and a Civil Aliens Corps hypothesis tested and failed doneA direct test of a hypothesis published an hour earlier: that the Crupi brothers, released from internment in late 1943, would have Civil Aliens Corps files in NAA series J1737 as Rosario Mazza does.THE HYPOTHESIS IS WRONG. The complete CRUPI name index — 233 items, all read — contains NO J1737 ITEM, NO «Civil Aliens Corps» and NO CAS service number for any Crupi. Rosario Mazza has one; neither of his wife's brothers does. Recorded as a negative rather than quietly dropped, because it was published as a plan and somebody would otherwise look for it again. WHAT IT MIGHT MEAN is unresolved: the Corps was not universal, and men released in October and December 1943 may simply have gone home to the cane. BUT THE SEARCH ANSWERED A BETTER QUESTION. ANTONINO CRUPI'S INTERNMENT DOSSIER IS NAA: MP1103/1, Q7838 — «Prisoner of War/Internee: Crupi, Antonio; Date of birth - 05 November 1907», Melbourne, 1942-1943, ACCESS OPEN; with its companion MP1103/2, Q7838. AND THE NUMBER CLOSES THE LOOP: his ALIENS REGISTRATION CARD, read this morning, carries «Q. 7838» beside his name. Same man, two series, one number. The identification no longer rests on birthplace alone. MARIO CRUPI LIKEWISE: MP1103/2, Q30124 — «Crupi, Mario; Year of birth - 1905». His aliens card gives «N.B.E. certificate Q30124». Same closure. A CONFLICT TO FLAG, NOT TO RESOLVE. The card gives Antonino's date of birth as «5.10.07» — 5 OCTOBER 1907 in Australian order. The dossier catalogue says «05 NOVEMBER 1907». One month apart, both from Commonwealth paperwork about the same man. The dossier itself would settle it; neither figure is preferred here. AND A THIRD ANTONIO CRUPI, WHO IS NOT OURS. MP1103/1 and MP1103/2, PWV11028: «Crupi, Antonio; Date of birth - 10 January 1904», a VICTORIAN internee — the PWV prefix against our Q. That is now the THIRD man of that name this archive has had to set aside, after the Serrata card at barcode 9209609 and the Silkwood East farmer on the 1959 roll. FINDING AID: NAA BP628/5, «Name Index and registration cards for internees dossiers Queensland - CACCIOLA to CURSIO», 1939-1962, Brisbane, OPEN. CRUPI falls inside that range, as MAZZA falls inside MACAUDO-MYSLIM.
20 Sep 2026NAA NameSearch — ARENA is 1650 items; ROCCO ARENA is nineteen, and one of them is ours doneNameSearch on ARENA across all record categories, then narrowed to the given name ROCCO.ARENA IS NOT SWEEPABLE BY SURNAME: 1,650 ITEMS. It is a common Sicilian and Calabrian name and it is also a place -- «Arena» is a comune in the province of Vibo Valentia, and a D4028 card in the results belongs to a MICHELE VARTULI born at «Arena Catongaro». Writing out 1,650 rows would be a waste and reading them for surnames would invent relatives. THE DISCRIMINATOR HAS TO BE A GIVEN NAME. ROCCO ARENA IS NINETEEN ITEMS AND EXACTLY ONE IS THIS FAMILY: NAA: J25 1971/3900, «Arena, Rocco; wife Angelina [or Angela] [nee Polistena]», Brisbane, 1953-1971. THAT IS HIS ONLY RECORD IN THE WHOLE NAME INDEX -- no aliens card, no naturalisation, no arrival item of his own. He landed in December 1953, after the wartime series closed and before the Commonwealth started keeping much else on assisted Italians, and the one file that names him names him as a husband. THE OTHER EIGHTEEN, AND THE ONE THAT MATTERS MOST IS AN INTERNMENT. A ROCCO ARENA WAS INTERNED IN AUSTRALIA: MP1103/1 and MP1103/2, PWN9406, «Date of birth - 31 AUGUST 1909»; an Aliens Tribunal objection, A367 C18000/298, «Objection No. 35»; C329 42, the same objection; C123 4326 and C123 ITALIAN INTERNEES [Box 611], both Sydney 1940; and ST1233/1 N35192, «Rocco Arena [Interned] [Application for Naturalisation]». HE IS NOT THIS FAMILY AND THE ARITHMETIC IS ABSOLUTE: this archive's Rocco Arena ARRIVED IN AUSTRALIA ON 27 DECEMBER 1953, thirteen years after that internment, and the Rocco Arena born at Scilla in 1861 DIED THERE ON 8 FEBRUARY 1932. Neither could have been in a Sydney camp in 1940. THIS ARCHIVE HAS JUST DOCUMENTED THREE INTERNED MEN IN THIS FAMILY, and a fourth of the right surname would be seized on. It is not one. THE REST, SO NOBODY RE-EXCLUDES THEM ONE BY ONE. A714 42/15084: Rocco Arena naturalised 25 June 1946, BORN GANZIRRI (Messina) 1907, resident Rose Bay NSW. SP11/5: arrived Sydney per REGINA D'ITALIA 3 October 1925. SP908/1 ITALIAN/ARENA ROCCO [1]: born 14 August 1942, arrived Sydney per ROMA 4 January 1963. SP908/1 [2]: born 6 September 1933, arrived Sydney per NEPTUNIA 3 October 1960. A435 1945/4/2718: born 3 February 1907. D4881: arrived Melbourne per GUGLIELMO MARCONI 2 March 1964, file at Adelaide. A1870 ARENA R: arrived Sydney per G MARCONI 13 May 1964. D400 SA1960/8987, D1915 SA17497, C3198 Z08102302J, B44 V1970/2215: South Australian, Sydney and Melbourne files without dates in their titles. SEVEN DISTINCT MEN AT LEAST, ON FIVE DIFFERENT BIRTH DATES, IN FIVE STATES. The surname plus the given name is still not an identification here; the arrival date is.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, the birth Tavole of 1834, 1835 and 1836, P sections read complete doneCAMILLO ANTONIO POLISTENA'S BIRTH. His son Giovanni's birth act of September 1883 makes him FORTY-EIGHT, so born about 1835, and his death act of 1924 names his parents GIUSEPPE POLISTENA and ANNA PONTILLO. Three years read across, not sampled -- 1834, 1835 and 1836 -- with the complete P section of each annual Tavola.HE IS NOT IN ANY OF THEM, AND THE SHAPE OF THE ABSENCE IS THE FINDING. 1834 (container wR8Jg9p, 114 images, Tavola 107-114, 201 births): the P section is entries 140-164 and holds Paladino, Patafio, Piria, Parisi, Papalia, Boscaro, Pirrotta, Pizzarello, Poleo. NOT ONE POLISTENA. 1835 (container wbb2o4g, 136 images, Tavola 130-136, 206 births): P is entries 137-153 -- Paladino, Parenti, Prini, Pirrotta, Parisi, Panella, Priocca, Papalia, Patafio, Pugliese. NOT ONE POLISTENA. 1836 (container w6rZ7KQ, 126 images, Tavola 119-126, 222 births): P is entries 167-192 and holds EXACTLY ONE -- entry 191, «POLISTINA ANNUNZIATA, di GIUSEPPE e MARTELLO ANNA, 2 Dicembre». THREE YEARS, 629 BIRTHS, ONE POLISTENA. The P section at Scilla in the 1830s is Paladino and Pirrotta and Patafio; the Polistena are barely there. THIS BEARS ON THE WHOLE QUARTER: the Polistena may be incomers to Scilla rather than an old family of it, which would explain why this archive has never been able to push them above Camillo Antonio. AND THE ONE ENTRY IS A LEAD WITH A FLAW. «Giuseppe e Martello Anna» has the right two given names for Camillo Antonio's parents -- GIUSEPPE and ANNA -- and THE WRONG SURNAME for the mother: the death act of 1924 says ANNA PONTILLO. Either that index line is wrong, or the death act is, or these are two different couples. THE TAVOLA IS A FINDING AID AND THIS ARCHIVE HAS BEEN CAUGHT BY ONE BEFORE -- the 1854 index that called Serafina Arlotta «Rosa» and briefly turned a brother into a cousin. ACT 191 OF 1836 WOULD SETTLE IT and it is three images away. WORTH NOTING IN PASSING: 1836 entry 168 is «PONTILLO GIOVANNI, di Gius.e e Paladino Gius.a» -- a Pontillo household at Scilla with a Giuseppe at its head, in the same year and the same section. Pontillo and Polistena sitting four entries apart is exactly the circumstance in which a clerk writes one for the other. READ THE SAME HOUR, AND IT IS ACT 204, NOT 191 -- the Tavola's «Num. d'ordine» is an INDEX ROW and that index carries no act number at all; act 191 is a Squillaci birth at Solano. ACT 204: 2 DECEMBER 1836, the midwife TERESA ARENA declaring a girl born that morning to ANNA PONTILLO, 35, wife of GIUSEPPE POLISTINA, 38, MARINARO, named ANNUNZIATA. THE INDEX WAS WRONG AND THE DEATH ACT WAS RIGHT: the mother is PONTILLO, and ANNUNZIATA IS CAMILLO ANTONIO'S SISTER. It also dates Giuseppe Polistena to about 1798 and Anna Pontillo to about 1801, and gives the Polistena a trade -- MARINARO. NEXT: then widen to 1830-1833 and 1837-1840, since a declared age is a range and this archive has already been burned reading three points inside a window as a search of the window.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla births 1830–1840, ELEVEN consecutive P sections read for Camillo Antonio Polistena doneELEVEN CONSECUTIVE YEARS, read across and not sampled: the complete P section of the annual birth Tavola for every year from 1830 to 1840. His son's 1883 birth act makes him forty-eight, so born about 1835, and his death act of 1924 names his parents GIUSEPPE POLISTENA and ANNA PONTILLO.HE IS IN NONE OF THE ELEVEN, AND THE ELEVEN MAP THE WHOLE SURNAME AT SCILLA. Every Polistena birth in those years, with parents: 1830 -- POLISTINA ANTONINO, di LUIGI e MARIANNA BAVIERA, 23 Febbraio; POLISTINA SALVADORE, di FRANCESCO e GIUSEPPA LONGARDO, 9 Maggio. 1831 -- POLISTINA FRANCESCO ANTONIO, di LUIGI e GIOVANNA BAVIERA, 21 Giugno. 1832 -- POLISTINA DOMENICA, di FRANCESCO e GIUSEPPA LONGORDO, 17 Dicembre. 1833 -- POLISTINA ROCCO, DI GIUSEPPE E PONTILLO ANNA, 13 Dicembre. 1834, 1835 -- NONE. 1836 -- POLISTINA ANNUNZIATA, DI GIUSEPPE E ANNA PONTILLO, 2 Dicembre (atto 204, read in full; the Tavola misnames the mother MARTELLO). 1837, 1838 -- NONE. 1839 -- POLISTINA MARIA, di FRANCESCO e GIUSEPPA LONGARDO, 30 Maggio. 1840 -- POLISTINA FORTUNATA, DI GIUSEPPE E PONTILLO ANNA, 28 Gennaro. THREE HOUSEHOLDS, EIGHT BIRTHS, ELEVEN YEARS, ABOUT 2,100 CHILDREN. Giuseppe x Anna Pontillo (this archive's couple): Rocco 1833, Annunziata 1836, FORTUNATA 1840. Francesco x Giuseppa Longardo: Salvadore 1830, Domenica 1832, Maria 1839. Luigi x Marianna/Giovanna Baviera: Antonino 1830, Francesco Antonio 1831. AND THE SPACING IS THE ARGUMENT. This archive's couple have children in DECEMBER 1833, DECEMBER 1836 and JANUARY 1840 -- three years apart, twice over, with nothing before or between. 1830, 1831 and 1832 are read and hold no child of theirs, so ROCCO IS THE ELDEST and Camillo Antonio CANNOT be earlier. HE MUST BE 1841 OR LATER. THE NAMING CUSTOM AGREES. A first son takes the father's father's name, so Giuseppe's father was a ROCCO POLISTENA; a later son takes the mother's father's, which would make CAMILLO ANTONIO a namesake of a CAMILLO PONTILLO. Both point the search forward, not back. SO «FORTY-EIGHT» IN SEPTEMBER 1883 IS WRONG BY AT LEAST SIX YEARS. Anna Pontillo was about thirty-five in 1836, so she could bear children to about 1846. THE WINDOW IS NOW 1841-1848, and no container for any of those years has been bought. ONE FREE CORROBORATION IN PASSING: the 1830 A section, entry 2, reads «ARLOTTA SERAFINA -- GIOVANNI, e COSTA MARIA -- 13 Giugno». That is Rocco Arena's mother, her date and both parents, confirming from the index what atto 89 of 1830 says in the act -- and confirming again that the «atto 2» this archive cited for years was a Tavola row number.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla births 1841–1843, the window after the eleven-year sweep partCAMILLO ANTONIO POLISTENA, continued forward. The eleven years 1830-1840 showed his parents' children falling three years apart with no room for him in front, so the window moved to 1841-1848. Complete P sections read for 1841 and 1842.TWO MORE YEARS CLEAR, A FOURTH HOUSEHOLD, AND A TRAP WORTH MORE THAN EITHER. 1841 (container 03bBOvJ, 124 images, Tavola 116-124, 218 births): the P block is entries 151-171 and holds Pontillo, Prini, Pirrotta, Bova, Paini, Patafio, Palmisano, Paladino, Pizzarello, Papalia, Piria, Porospero. NO POLISTENA AT ALL. 1842 (container 58rKdQN, 127 images, Tavola 120-127, 222 births): the P block is entries 144-173 and holds EXACTLY ONE -- entry 145, «POLISTINA SALVADORE, di DOM.co e COSTA CATERINA, 12 Gennaro». That is a FOURTH Polistena household: DOMENICO POLISTINA and CATERINA COSTA. Not Giuseppe and Anna Pontillo. THE TRAP IS IN 1841, ENTRY 160: «PALMISANO GIO. BATTISTA -- GIUSEPPE e ANNA PONTILLO -- 26 Agosto». A GIUSEPPE MARRIED TO AN ANNA PONTILLO, in the right town in the right decade, four letters away from GIUSEPPE POLISTENA and ANNA PONTILLO -- and the surname is PALMISANO. Anybody scanning the parents column of these Tavole for «Giuseppe e Anna Pontillo» would stop here and be wrong. It is either a second Anna Pontillo or the same woman in a second marriage, and nothing here decides which. THE DISCRIMINATOR IS THE CHILD'S SURNAME, which is the one column a parent-scan skips. 1843 IS A DOUBLED YEAR AND THE INDEX IS IN THE OTHER ARK. an_ua2002874 (container wr9redO, 107 images) holds the acts and closes at act 200 with the sindaco's statement and NO TAVOLA. The second ark, an_ua2002877, is where the INDICE will be -- the same split this archive already met in 1852 and 1875. ITS CONTAINER IS NOT BOUGHT: the lookup returned «403 Forbidden», caught by the control test and recorded as a block, not as a missing register. STILL TO READ: 1843 (needs the second ark's container), 1844, 1845, 1846, 1847, 1848. Containers for 1841, 1842 and 1843's act volume are saved.
20 Sep 2026Trove — attempted and BLOCKED BY THE TOOL, not by the archive wallRow 79: sweep Trove's newspaper and gazette holdings for all ten surnames, chasing naturalisation notices, which give a date, a street address and a former nationality.NOT DONE, AND THE REASON IS WORTH RECORDING PRECISELY BECAUSE IT IS NOT A NEGATIVE. Trove's search page LOADS and ACCEPTS the query -- it echoes «l-artType: newspapers / All of these words: Prostamo» back onto the page -- and then NEVER RENDERS A RESULT LIST. No count, no articles, no empty-state message. THE CONSOLE SAYS WHY: «TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'clear')» thrown twice from Trove's own vendor bundle, chunk-vendors.af8ce3cc.js. That is a STORAGE OR CACHE API the browser pane does not provide, and the single-page app dies on it before it paints results. IT IS A TOOLING INCOMPATIBILITY, NOT A BLOCK AND NOT AN ABSENCE. Recording it as «wall» rather than as zero results, because a searcher meeting a blank Trove page could very easily write down «nothing for Prostamo in Australian newspapers», which would be false -- Trove has ALREADY given this archive Brisbane Prostamo naturalisations and Maria Assunta's gazettal of 4 July 1966 at 3 Frank Street, Graceville, both found by other means. THE TWO WAYS ROUND, NEITHER OF WHICH NEEDS ANOTHER ATTEMPT AT THE SPA. (1) THE TROVE API v3: free, keyed, api.trove.nla.gov.au, and it returns JSON that needs no JavaScript at all -- a key is requested from the NLA in a few minutes and is the right long-term answer for a ten-surname sweep. (2) DAVID'S OWN BROWSER, where the page works.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Nati 1833 atto 174, read in the original doneRocco Polistina's birth act, to confirm the 1833 Tavola line and get his parents' ages from a second independent document. Found by date, since that index carries no act number: three probes bracketing 13 December.READ, AND IT DOES THREE THINGS. ONE, IT CONFIRMS THE PARENTS FROM A SECOND ACT. GIUSEPPE POLISTINA, THIRTY-FOUR, MARINARO, and ANNA PONTILLO, THIRTY-THREE, in December 1833; against thirty-eight and thirty-five in December 1836. Three years of register, four and two of declared age -- the ordinary slop of this register, and both acts land on Giuseppe born 1798-99 and Anna born 1800-01. No conflict. TWO, THE SAME MIDWIFE DELIVERED BOTH CHILDREN. TERESA ARENA, thirty-eight here and forty-four in 1836, LEVATRICE, declared both births in person and produced the witnesses at both. THREE, AND IT IS THE ONE WORTH CARRYING: THE MARGIN CARRIES A MARRIAGE ADDED THIRTY-SIX YEARS LATER. «L'anno 1869, il giorno 12 Luglio -- il sottoscritto Uffiziale dello Stato Civile dichiara che ROCCO POLISTINA, cui ha rapporto il contro scritto atto di nascita, HA CONTRATTO MATRIMONIO IN QUESTO COMUNE CON COLORINA BRI[...] nel giorno 12 andante mese ed anno. L'Uffiziale G. Zagari. Reggio 26 Agosto 1869.» So he lived to thirty-five and married at Scilla on 12 JULY 1869. The bride's surname is cut by the binding and her forename reads COLORINA or COLOMBA; recorded as read and flagged, because the 1869 marriage act will give it plainly and this archive has been burned by a name guessed off an index before. THE METHOD POINT: SCILLA'S RESTAURAZIONE BIRTH ACTS CARRY MARGINAL ANNOTATIONS ADDED DECADES LATER, and this archive has never looked at one. A birth act is not only a birth. Camillo Antonio Polistena's own act, when it is found, may carry his marriage in the same hand -- and so may the Arena and Arlotta birth acts already read, whose margins nobody has examined.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — three Scilla birth-act MARGINS read, and the annotation rule calibrated doneRow 154, opened an hour earlier: Scilla birth acts carry marginal annotations added decades later, and this archive had never looked at one. Three margins tested -- Rocco Polistina 1833, Serafina Arlotta 1830, Filippo Arena 1852 -- on containers already bought.ONE ANNOTATED, TWO BLANK, AND THE RULE IS NARROWER THAN IT LOOKED. ROCCO POLISTINA, Nati 1833 atto 174: margin carries his marriage AT SCILLA on 12 JULY 1869, signed and dated from Reggio six weeks later. SERAFINA ARLOTTA, Nati 1830 atto 89: MARGIN EMPTY. She married at Scilla on 7 February 1851. FILIPPO ARENA, Nati 1852 atto 48: MARGIN EMPTY. Any marriage of his would fall after 1866. THE READING THAT FITS ALL THREE: the annotation is an ITALIAN-ERA habit, absent from an 1851 marriage and present on an 1869 one, AND it is written only when the later act is registered in the SAME COMUNE -- the note says «ha contratto matrimonio IN QUESTO COMUNE» in so many words. SO A BLANK MARGIN IS WEAK EVIDENCE. For a pre-1866 marriage it means nothing whatever. For a post-1866 one it is consistent with a marriage somewhere else -- which for Filippo Arena is the reading the rest of the evidence already supports. TWO ACTS READ IN THE REGISTER FOR THE FIRST TIME AS A BY-PRODUCT. NATI 1830 ATTO 89 confirms that the certified copy bound into the 1851 processetti is FAITHFUL word for word -- worth knowing, since a Pizzo extract once lost ten years off a man's age. NATI 1852 ATTO 48, Filippo Arena's birth, carried here until today off the Tavola alone: 14 FEBRUARY 1852, before GAETANO FERRANTE, sindaco; ANTONIO ARENA, di anni TRENTASEI, MARINARO, and SERAFINA ARLOTTA, di anni VENTOTTO; witnesses ROCCO PIZZARELLO, 30, FERRARO, and TOMMASO CARDONA, 30, FALEGNAME; THE DECLARANT COULD NOT WRITE. AND ITS AGES ARE THE WORST THIS ARCHIVE HAS SEEN IN ONE LINE: he was thirty-two and she was twenty-one. FOUR YEARS AND SEVEN YEARS OUT. Two years later the same couple are entered as thirty-four and twenty-two, which fits. The clerk guessed. This is now the act to point at whenever a declared age is about to identify somebody.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Nati 1853 atto 53 and the margins of 1852-1853, read in the original doneRow 154: check the margins of Scilla birth acts already fetched. Started with «Domenico Arena, Nati 1853 atto 2», one of the three brothers who vanish after 1898.THE MARGIN WAS THE SMALL FIND. THE ACT WAS THE BIG ONE. ACT 2 OF 1853 IS NOT HIM. It is DOMENICA BELLANTONI, born 4 January to Maria Bellantoni, filatrice, twenty-nine, who acknowledged her as her natural daughter. «2» was a TAVOLA ROW NUMBER -- the second entry in the A block -- exactly as Serafina Arlotta's «atto 2» of 1830 turned out to be act 89 earlier today. Both are «atto 2» because a surname-ordered A block puts Arena and Arlotta second. THE REAL ACT IS 53, FOUND BY DATE IN FOUR PROBES -- and it says the child is a GIRL. «FRANCESCANTONIO ARENA, DI ANNI TRENTADUE, MARINARO ... ci à presentato UNA FEMINA ... nata da SERAFINA ARLOTTA, sua moglie legittima, DI ANNI VENTIDUE ... di dare alla FANCIULLA il nome di DOMENICA», and the Indicazione column records the baptism of DOMENICA ARENA. THREE STATEMENTS IN ONE ACT. SO ROCCO ARENA'S «BROTHER DOMENICO» IS HIS SISTER DOMENICA, and this archive carried her as a son for fourteen weeks without opening the act. AND THE AGES IN THIS ACT ARE EXACT -- thirty-two and twenty-two, both landing on the known birth dates -- fourteen months after the 1852 act made the same couple thirty-six and twenty-eight. Individual acts are right or wrong; the register is not drifting. THE MARGINS, WITH A CONTROL THIS TIME. 1852 act 55: «Atto 18 Febbraio 1881 N. 11 -- SCARFONE ROCCO contrasse matrimonio in Scilla con VITA MARIA». 1853 act 54: a marriage of 20 December 1876. 1853 act 55: «PALADINO SANTA contrasse matrimonio in Scilla», 8 September 1883. SO THE PRACTICE IS LIVE IN BOTH VOLUMES -- and FILIPPO ARENA'S MARGIN (1852 act 48) IS EMPTY. That upgrades an hour-old grading: a blank margin here is evidence, not nothing. HE DID NOT MARRY AT SCILLA. ONE CORRECTION OF MY OWN, MADE AND THEN UNMADE WITHIN THE HOUR: I first took writing at the right edge of the 1852 act-48 opening for act 49's margin. It is act 49's BAPTISM column. The control had to be run properly on a different opening before the blank meant anything.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Nati 1860 atto 45, and the annotation inference disproved correctionRow 154 continued: Giuseppe Arena's birth act, one of the two brothers who vanish after 1898, and a man KNOWN to have married at Scilla on 12 September 1885. A control case for the annotation rule, chosen because the answer was already known.THE CONTROL FAILS THE RULE, AND THE RULE IS WITHDRAWN. Act 45 of 1860 is Giuseppe Arena's birth -- the Tavola number is the act number here, unlike 1830 and 1853 -- declared 6 MARCH 1860 by the midwife GIUSEPPA DE GIROLAMO, twenty-three, for a boy born the fifth at ten o'clock to SERAFINA ARLOTTA, twenty-nine, and FRANCESCO ANTONIO ARENA, marinaro. The date matches this archive exactly and Serafina's age is exact. AND THE ANNOTATION BOX IS EMPTY. GIUSEPPE ARENA MARRIED MARIA MARTELLO AT SCILLA ON 12 SEPTEMBER 1885 -- act 73, read here in the original -- and nothing was ever written back into his birth act. So a blank box does NOT mean a man did not marry in the comune, and the inference drawn against FILIPPO ARENA an hour earlier is withdrawn in full. Filippo's blank margin now means nothing at all. WHAT SURVIVES OF ROW 154: a PRESENT annotation is still free evidence -- Rocco Polistina's marriage of 12 July 1869 came that way, and marriages of 1876, 1881 and 1883 sit on other acts. IT IS A BONUS WHEN IT IS THERE AND SILENCE WHEN IT IS NOT. AND THE BOX MOVES BETWEEN VOLUMES. 1852 and 1853 use a LEFT column headed «MARGINE per scriversi alcune notizie riguardanti gli atti di Nascita». 1860 uses a RIGHT box headed «MENZIONI prescritte dagli articoli 64, 65 e 106, LL. CC.». Same purpose, opposite side of the page. Anybody reporting «the margin is blank» must say which side they looked at. ONE MORE CAUTION SOFTENED: this archive records that when a LEVATRICE declares, the act gives the parents WITHOUT ages. Here a levatrice declared and both ages are given. A tendency, not a rule.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla births 1843, 1844 and 1845, completing the window round Camillo Antonio Polistena partThree more P sections after the eleven-year sweep of 1830-1840 and the 1841-1842 pass. With these, the Scilla birth index has been read for SIXTEEN CONSECUTIVE YEARS, 1830 to 1845.HE IS IN NONE OF THEM, AND HIS PARENTS' CHILDBEARING AT SCILLA IS NOW BOUNDED. 1843 (container LD6b47G -- the SECOND ark of a doubled year; an_ua2002874 holds the acts and closes at act 200 with no index, an_ua2002877 carries the Tavola at images 16-21, badly soiled): the P block holds Pizzarello, Papalia, Paladino, Princi, Patafio, Pirrotta, Perina, Piccone, Porchi, Pontillo, Poleo. NO POLISTINA AS A CHILD. One as a MOTHER, for the third time: «Pirrotta Antonia -- Rocco e POLISTINA ANNUNZIATA». 1844 (container LNolOVK, 111 images, Tavola 104-111, 192 births): TWO Polistina, entries 131 and 133 -- «POLISTINA ROCCO, di DOM.co e COSTA CAROLINA» and «POLISTINA GIUSEPPE, di FRAN.co e LONGORDO GIUSEPPA». Both are the OTHER households. Neither is Giuseppe x Anna Pontillo. 1845 (container 5gGp2Rz, 120 images, Tavola 114-120, 210 births): PARTIAL. The P block runs entries 145-170 and SEVEN OF THE EIGHTEEN LINES ARE TOO FAINT TO READ -- 145, 147, 150, 151, 153, 154 and 157. No Polistina among the legible ones, but that is NOT a clean negative and is not recorded as one. The volume needs a second look at higher contrast. SO THE SHAPE OF THE FAMILY IS NOW FIXED. GIUSEPPE POLISTENA and ANNA PONTILLO have children at Scilla in DECEMBER 1833, DECEMBER 1836 and JANUARY 1840 -- and in the twelve other years read, NOTHING. Anna was about thirty-five in 1836, so her childbearing plausibly ends around 1840-42, which is exactly what the register shows. AND THAT LEAVES A CONTRADICTION THIS ARCHIVE SHOULD STATE RATHER THAN SMOOTH. Camillo Antonio Polistena's death act of 1924 names these two as his parents. His son's birth act of 1883 makes him forty-eight. THERE IS NO YEAR IN THEIR CHILDBEARING WINDOW WHERE HE FITS, AND THE WINDOW HAS BEEN READ ACROSS. Four readings, none preferred here: (1) he was born somewhere else and the family came to Scilla; (2) the 1924 death act's parents are wrong; (3) the Tavola omitted him -- these indexes DO drop entries, and 1898's death index carries an Arena with no act number at all; (4) he is one of the children already found under a name this archive has not connected. THE TEST THAT WOULD SEPARATE THEM is his MARRIAGE act to Giovanna Romano, about 1870-1880, which would give his age a third time and his birthplace outright. Scilla's Italian-era marriages 1866-1913 are all on Antenati and the series is already listed in scilla-arks-italiano.tsv.

NO POLISTENA. 1882 (container wErAXdW, 40 images, Indice at 39-40): TWO POLISTINA, AND NEITHER IS HIM. «POLISTINA GIUSEPPE x ZAGARI GIOVANNA» -- the register number reads 5 or 57 and is not certain at this resolution -- and «POLISTINA LUIGI CARMELO x MATRA NUNZIATA», act 48. THAT IS WORTH MORE THAN A NEGATIVE. Seven years of this index have now been read and 1882 is the FIRST to hold the surname at all -- so the Polistena were marrying at Scilla by 1882, and the earlier silence is not an artefact of the index. AND «LUIGI CARMELO» IS A NAME THIS ARCHIVE HAS MET: the 1831 birth Tavola gives «Polistina Francesco Antonio, di LUIGI e Giovanna Baviera», the third of the four Scilla Polistena households. A Luigi Carmelo marrying in 1882 fits that line as a son or grandson and fits nothing here. SO: 1874, 1876, 1877, 1878, 1879, 1880 and 1882 READ. 1875 has no index. 1881's container lookup returned 403 twice and is not bought. Left: 1870-1873, 1881, and 1875 by acts. THE TWO 1882 ACTS ARE WORTH OPENING ANYWAY -- a Scilla marriage act of that date gives both spouses' ages, BOTH BIRTHPLACES and all four parents, so either one would say whether the Polistena of Scilla were born there or came in, which is the question row 137 could not answer from sixteen years of births. NAVIGATION NOTE FOR THE 1882 VOLUME, BOUGHT WITH FOUR WASTED PROBES. Container wErAXdW, 40 images. IT IS SPLIT INTO TWO SERIES AND BOTH ARE NUMBERED FROM ONE. Image 33 carries a margin reading «Numero 4 -- Colosi Giuseppe Luigi Vincenzo / Zagari Giuseppa Maria Raffaela», which is PARTE II act 4, not parte I act 4 -- parte II begins around image 31 and is the register of marriages celebrated elsewhere and transcribed here. The INDICE at images 39-40 gives a «Numero di Registro» WITHOUT SAYING WHICH PART, so an index number cannot be resolved to an image without checking both series. Several images in the thirties are BLANK VERSOS showing only bleed-through. THE CHEAP WAY IN, FOR WHOEVER CONTINUES: every act carries a MARGIN LABEL giving its number and BOTH SPOUSES' NAMES, so a montage of the narrow left margin strip across a run of images identifies every act in the volume without reading a single act body. That is the same trick that worked on the 1874 marriage volume and it should have been used here first.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Matrimoni 1881 atto 4: Camillo Antonio Polistena x Giovanna Romano, FOUND doneRow 155, closed. Eight years of the Scilla marriage index read; 1881's Indice Annuale gave the entry and the margin-strip montage gave the image in two calls.FOUND. SCILLA, MATRIMONI 1881, ATTO 4 -- 23 JANUARY 1881 at six seventeen in the evening. POLISTINA CAMILLO ANTONIO, DI ANNI TRENTASEI, MARINARO, NATO IN QUESTO COMUNE, figlio del FU GIUSEPPE e della FU ANNA PONTILLO. ROMANO GIOVANNA, DI ANNI VENTISEI, FILATRICE, NATA IN QUESTO COMUNE, figlia di ANNUNZIATO e di ANGELA ZIRILLI, both living. THE AGE IS THE FINDING. THIRTY-SIX in January 1881 is a birth about 1844 -- and his son's birth act of September 1883 makes him FORTY-EIGHT, a birth about 1835. NINE YEARS APART. The 1835 figure is what this archive swept eleven years of birth Tavole around, and the answer was never in them. THE MARRIAGE ACT IS PREFERRED, on the rule this archive has already applied to Giovanni Arlotta and Maria Costa: a man appearing in person to marry, with the parish papers checked, against a father registering a newborn in the evening. HE WAS BORN AT SCILLA -- «nato in questo Comune» -- so the birth act EXISTS and the sixteen-year sweep missed it. AND 1844 IS INSIDE THAT SWEEP: the 1844 P block was read for entries 127-138 WITHOUT ESTABLISHING WHERE THE BLOCK ENDS, and 1845 is on record as PARTIAL with seven of eighteen lines too faint. Those two years are now the shortest road left, and they are years already paid for. THREE MORE THINGS THE ACT GIVES. GIUSEPPE POLISTENA AND ANNA PONTILLO WERE BOTH DEAD BY JANUARY 1881 -- the first bound either has had, against their last sighting alive in January 1840. The parentage is confirmed from a SECOND document, forty-three years before the 1924 death act. And GIOVANNA ROMANO is dated -- twenty-six, born at Scilla about 1854 -- with ANNUNZIATO ROMANO and ANGELA ZIRILLI both alive, which is the FOURTH record to call her a Romano and the first in which she is the subject. METHOD: the margin-strip montage found the act in two calls. Images 2-7 carry acts 1, 3, 5, 7, 9, 11 in the left margin with BOTH SPOUSES' NAMES, so act 4 is the right-hand act of image 3. The same trick that had been written down, ignored, and then vindicated on the 1882 volume an hour earlier.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Nati 1844 Tavola entry 142: Camillo Antonio Polistena's birth, FOUND doneRow 137, closed. His marriage act of January 1881 made him thirty-six, pointing at 1844 -- a year already read and recorded as clear. It was not clear; the reading was.HE IS AT ENTRY 142 OF THE 1844 TAVOLA: «POLISTINA CAMILLO -- GIUSEPPE e PONTILLO ANNA -- 21 SETTEMBRE». BORN AT SCILLA ON 21 SEPTEMBER 1844. THE ARITHMETIC IS EXACT, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES IT SAFE. He was THIRTY-SIX YEARS AND FOUR MONTHS at his marriage on 23 January 1881, and the act says «trentasei». He was THIRTY-EIGHT AND ELEVEN MONTHS at his son's birth on 16 September 1883, and that act says FORTY-EIGHT -- ten years wrong, and the figure this archive searched around for sixteen years. AND THE REASON IT TOOK SO LONG IS MINE. The 1844 P block was read EARLIER THE SAME DAY. The crop covered entries 127 to 138 and stopped; the row was written saying that the end of the block had not been established, and then 1845, 1830-1832 and five marriage years were swept instead of spending one more crop on the year already open. HE IS FOUR ENTRIES PAST WHERE I STOPPED. THE RULE THIS BUYS, AND IT IS THE SECOND OF ITS KIND HERE: A PARTIAL READING RECORDED AS A NEGATIVE IS THE SAME ERROR AS A 403 RECORDED AS A NIL. This archive already controls for the second. Where a section is read, FIND ITS END -- the next section letter -- before writing «not there». 1845 is still on record as PARTIAL with seven faint lines and should be finished on the same principle. A FIFTH POLISTENA HOUSEHOLD CAME WITH IT: entry 143, the very next line, is «POLISTINA TOMMASO -- D. ANTONIO e PALADINO ANNUNZIATA», the «D.» being DON. NEXT, AND CHEAP: the ACT. The Tavola carries no act number and the register runs in date order, so 21 September 1844 is two or three probes into container LNolOVK. The act would give BOTH PARENTS' AGES -- dating Giuseppe Polistena and Anna Pontillo a third time -- and may carry a marriage annotation in its margin. THE ACT ITSELF WAS HUNTED THE SAME HOUR AND NOT FOUND -- FIVE ACTS ELIMINATED, WRITTEN DOWN SO NOBODY CHECKS THEM AGAIN. The 1844 volume maps cleanly: image N carries folio N-2, with the EVEN act on the left page and the ODD act on the right. Image 73 = folio 71 = acts 140 and 141; image 74 = folio 72 = acts 142 and 143. CHECKED AND EXCLUDED, all in the September run: ACT 138 (declarant Domenico Spoletti, child Alberto...), ACT 139 (child Giuseppe), ACT 140 (declarant Cosmo Bellantoni, child MARIANNA VIOLANTE), ACT 141 (declarant Domenico Calarco, baptised at the parish OF SOLANO), ACT 142 (declarant Giovanni Macri, mother a Barbaro of thirty, child ROSANTONIO, born the 28th). NONE IS POLISTINA. SO EITHER THE ACT SITS OUTSIDE 138-142 -- the September acts plausibly run 135 to 145 -- or the Tavola's «21 Settembre» is the BIRTH date against a later declaration, or that faint line reads a different day. THE FINDING DOES NOT DEPEND ON IT: the Tavola gives the parents and the date, and the marriage act's «trentasei» is exact for 21 September 1844 to the month. But THE ACT HAS NOT BEEN READ, and this archive's own rule says the index is not the source. NEXT PASS: montage the act-number-and-date band (region pct:50,10,48,20 at width 900 works) across images 68-76, which covers acts 132 to 149 in one look, instead of opening them one at a time as I did. AND THEN IT WAS FOUND, WITH THE TWO MONTAGES THAT SHOULD HAVE COME FIRST. One montage of the EVEN acts across images 68-77 mapped the year: September runs acts 129-143, October begins at 144. One montage of the ODD acts across images 68-73 put a POLISTINA at ACT 137. ATTO 137 OF 1844: «e comparso GIUSEPPE POLISTINA, DI ANNI CINQUANTA, MARINARO ... quale ci a presentato un MASCHIO ... nato da ANNA PONTILLO, FILATRICE, sua moglie legittima ... NEL GIORNO VENTUNO CORRENTE MESE ... di dare al fanciullo IL NOME DI CAMILLO ANTONIO.» The Indicazione column names the baptism of CAMILLO ANTONIO POLISTINA. SO THE TAVOLA WAS RIGHT ABOUT THE DATE AND THE PARENTS AND WRONG ABOUT THE NUMBER -- 142 was its row, 137 is the act -- WHICH IS THE THIRD TIME TODAY. And the act adds the full given name, CAMILLO ANTONIO, where the index gives only «Camillo». AND THE MARGIN CARRIES HIS MARRIAGE, annotated «Scilla 25 Gennaio 1881» -- two days after the wedding of 23 January 1881. The birth act and the marriage act point at each other across thirty-seven years. FIVE ACTS WERE OPENED ONE AT A TIME BEFORE THE MONTAGES WERE USED. That is the whole lesson: the montage is not a last resort, it is the first move in any volume whose index does not give act numbers.

20 Sep 2026Antenati — the act-head montage, proved on three unindexed marriage volumes doneMethod. A restauration and napoleonic marriage opening puts the END of one act on the left page and the HEAD of the next on the right: «Num. d'Ordine», the date, the groom's name, his age, his trade and his father. Cropping pct:52,18,46,26 at width 900 and tiling ten to a sheet reads a whole volume in four or five images.IT WORKS, AND IT REPLACES THE ANNUAL TAVOLA. 111 acts across 1815, 1816 and 1817 read in fourteen montages and three single crops. An unindexed volume is no longer a forty-opening read; it is a twenty-minute one. THE LIMIT: the crop shows the GROOM and his father, not the bride -- a search for a woman's marriage still needs the Tavola or a second crop band.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — twenty-one Scilla marriage containers, 1820-1843, banked in one sitting doneRow 157. The expensive step in any Antenati sweep is ark to container, and it normally costs a page load apiece against a limiter that 403s after about three. Fetched from inside the origin in page context, six at a time, five seconds apart.TWENTY-ONE CONTAINERS AND NO 403, where twenty-one navigations would have locked the session out repeatedly. Image counts came free off the IIIF manifests, which are never throttled. Every Scilla marriage volume from 1820 to 1843 is now openable without spending another page load -- which is what makes a Tavola sweep for Filippo Arena's siblings, and for Francesco Antonio's elder brothers, a reading job rather than a rate-limit job.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla processetti 1815, bundle 29: the Arena-Costa marriage file, READ ENTIRE doneRow 159. Container LqA4a7D, 364 images covering 1815 and 1816. 20 Sep 2026The archive's own chart — /tree/ now draws people the family tree does not contain doneRows 51 and 63. The blocker was recorded as «the charts are the shared kit's and this archive leaves the kit alone». Re-read the kit instead of working around it.THE KIT HAD ALREADY LEFT THE DOOR OPEN AND SAID SO IN A COMMENT. `lineage()` takes `parentsOf(row)` from the caller, and `kit/lib/ahnen.js` reads: «returning a row that is not in the file is allowed and is the point: a parent known only as a name on a register is still a real thing the chart should draw, and refusing it would quietly shorten the line.» So `/tree/`'s own parentsOf now falls back to `documented-parents.json` where the export gives a person no parents -- NOT where it gives them parents, because the chart is a drawing of the tree and silently redrawing it would be lying about the source. ROSARIO MAZZA'S LINE NO LONGER ENDS AT A CARD SAYING «Before Piedimonte Etneo, 1850»: MARIANO MAZZA and MARIA CATALANO now stand above him, graded as documented, unlinked because they have no person page. Nothing in the kit was touched and no fork was made.

20 Sep 2026Antenati — Piedimonte Etneo, Nati 1880 and 1881 read for Maria Nicotra doneRow 161. Both annual indexes read at the N section in the original; 1881's N block is split across two openings and both halves were read, because a section that ends at the foot of a page is exactly where a partial reading turns into a false negative.TWELVE CONSECUTIVE YEARS NOW, 1870-1881, AND STILL NO MARIA NICOTRA. 1880's N section is a single entry, NUCIFORA FRANCESCO 92 -- no Nicotra at all. 1881's is NICOTRA ANTONINA 24 at the foot of one opening, continuing on the next with NUCIFORA CONCETTA, NUCIFORA AGATA, NICOTRA MARIANO and NERI CONCETTA. Leonarda Bonanno's father is Gaetano Bonanno. That act may be another child of Rosario and Leonarda, or another of the town's Mazza households; it has not been opened and nothing is claimed. AND THE REACH IS NOW KNOWN, AND IT WAS NEVER HER TOWN. These indexes cover births registered AT PIEDIMONTE ETNEO and nothing else. Maria Nicotra was born at MASCALI on 8 December 1875 (Nati 1875 atto 144) to Giovanni Nicotra, a Mascali man, and Venera Cassaniti. Fourteen consecutive Piedimonte indexes were read carefully and could never have held her. THE NIL IS A FACT ABOUT THE COMUNE THAT WAS SEARCHED, not about the family.
20 Sep 2026NAA NameSearch — MAZZA Maria and NICOTRA, all records doneRow 161, asking the other side of the question. Twelve years of Piedimonte birth indexes have not produced her; her own Australian papers would give her birthplace directly, without another Italian register.FOUND, AND IT IS THE RIGHT INSTRUMENT: **NAA: BP25/1, ITALIAN MAZZA M**, Brisbane, Item ID 5041955, ACCESS OPEN -- «MAZZA [NEE NICOTRA], MARIA - Nationality: Italian - arrived Sydney 20 April 1962 on PHDCG». An Australian government file that states her MAIDEN NAME and gives an arrival this archive did not have. Alien registration files of this kind routinely carry a DATE AND PLACE OF BIRTH, which is exactly what twelve years of Piedimonte indexes have failed to establish. IT IS NOT DIGITISED -- paper, at the Brisbane reading room -- so it is a copy request, not a search. The NICOTRA name search returns 148 items; nothing else in the first twenty is this household, though «BP9/3, Nicotra, Teresa born 1895, arrived Brisbane on ORMONDE 16 September 1930» is a Brisbane Nicotra worth a second look.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — Piedimonte Etneo, Nati 1880 atto 66: the lead left standing is a son doneThe 1880 M section carried «Mazza Gaetano -- 66» and the row was written up as a lead, not a finding, because Gaetano is a common name and the town holds three Mazza households. 20 Sep 2026Antenati — the Riveli di anime e di terre, Piedimonte 1747-1748: the whole index read doneRow 162, and the first time this archive has opened anything from the Deputazione del Regno. Container noPNme4, 73 images. IMAGES 1-14 ARE AN ALPHABETICAL INDEX with cut letter tabs down the fore-edge, and it is alphabetised BY GIVEN NAME, so the whole of it has to be read -- which it was, all fourteen openings, about eighty households.THERE IS NO MAZZA AT PIEDIMONTE IN 1747. There are two MAZZEO: «Onofrio Mazzeo, folio 5» and «Silvestro Mazzeo, folio 29». Both were cropped at native resolution to be sure of the ending, and both read -EO. MAZZEO IS NOT MAZZA UNTIL SOMETHING SAYS IT IS -- Sicilian surname endings drift and the two may well be one family, but that is a hypothesis and this archive has been burned by exactly this kind of near-match before. THE REST OF THE TOWN IS STRIKINGLY CONTINUOUS: Pagano, Currenti, Grasso, Musumeci, Vasta, Puglisi, Torrisi, Sullo, Cadili, Vecchio, Barone, Moschella, di Mauro, Ricceri, Cassisi, Tomarchio, Zappala, Messina, Rinaldo, di Costa, Patane -- the same surnames that fill the birth indexes of the 1870s and 1880s. So the register is legible, the town is recognisable, and MAZZA IS NOT IN IT. That is consistent with what the nineteenth-century registers already said: no Mazza in the births of 1844 or 1846, two in 1845, four marriages in sixteen years.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — the two Mazzeo folios of the 1747 Piedimonte riveli, read in full doneRow 162. The index gave «Onofrio Mazzeo folio 5» and «Silvestro Mazzeo folio 29». The folio numbers are written in the top outer corner of each opening with an older number struck through, so the map falls out of five probes: FOLIO = IMAGE - 16 in container noPNme4.BOTH READ, AND THE SURNAME IS SETTLED: MAZZEO, written four times across two folios in two hands, never Mazza. FOLIO 5: «Rivelo che Io ONOFRIO MAZZEO ... in virtu di Bando promulgato sotto li 30 Aprile 1747. ANIME: Notato Onofrio Mazzeo d'anni TRENTA; et CARMINA moglie; et GIUSEPPE figlio.» FOLIO 29: «Rivelo che Io SILVESTRO MAZZEO ... ANIME: M.ro Silvestro Mazzeo d'anni TRENTACINQUE; et MARIA moglie», followed by his house -- «un loggio terraneo di pietra a cruda, scagliato dentro e di fuori di calce, per proprio abitare, consistente in una stanza in piano». THE SHAPE OF A RIVELO, AND ITS LIMIT: «Rivelo che Io ...» naming the declarant and the bando, then ANIME, then BENI STABILI in close detail, then the dues. AT PIEDIMONTE ONLY THE HEAD'S AGE IS GIVEN -- the wife and the son are named and not aged -- so a rivelo here will not hand you a wife's or a child's birth year, which is the thing one hopes for. It gives the HOUSEHOLD and the HOUSE.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — Briatico relisted, and the listing is NOT complete partTurning the Piedimonte lesson on the other deep line: `briatico-arks.tsv` holds EIGHTY registers, and nobody had checked whether that is the holding or just what was looked for. Prompted by CATERINA MELLUSO, born at Briatico in 1860 -- a wall on the ancestry chart with no parents and not one record against her.BRIATICO HAS 555 REGISTERS, NOT EIGHTY. The result facets read «Archivio di Stato di Catanzaro (440)» and «Archivio di Stato di Vibo Valentia (115)». A listing at s_size=500 returned 500 of them and showed years running 1810 to 1930 WITH NOTHING BETWEEN 1859 AND 1910 -- which would put Caterina Melluso's 1860 birth and her marriage to Alfonso Prostamo beyond Antenati entirely. THAT IS NOT YET A FINDING AND IS RECORDED AS PART, NOT DONE. FIFTY-FIVE REGISTERS WERE NOT IN THE SAMPLE, and the ones held at Vibo Valentia rather than Catanzaro are exactly where a later series would sit. A 500-of-555 listing reported as a gap is the same error as a partial index section reported as a negative, and this archive has paid for that twice. s_size above 500 times out and the rate limiter closed the session before it could be finished by year probes. TO FINISH: one listing that reaches all 555, or year probes on 1860, 1880 and 1900.

20 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Matrimoni 1813-1814 read entire, and an Arena household nobody had met doneRows 157 and 167. Container 0MnkW4l, two years in one volume, forty-four acts, read groom by groom off the act head.NO DONATO GROOM IN EITHER YEAR -- so with 1809, 1815, 1816 and 1817, SIX YEARS ARE NOW CLEAR FOR CANDELORO DONATO. 1810, 1811 and 1812 are NOT: their Tavole were read for the letter F on the Arena hunt, and a Candeloro sits under C or D. AND ACT 21 IS AN ARENA: DIEGO ARENA, thirty-seven, SARTORE, «figlio maggiore di DOMENICO ARENA, di anni SETTANTA, di professione BARBIERE, e di ANNA MELIDONI», marrying Maria Giuseppa Pontillo, sixteen, on 12 August 1813. DOMENICO ARENA WAS BORN ABOUT 1743, earlier than any Arena in this archive including Orazio, and he is a BARBER where the spine is sailors. 20 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, NINE consecutive marriage years read for a Donato: 1809-1817 doneRow 167. The three indexed napoleonic years, 1810, 1811 and 1812, were read WHOLE rather than for one letter -- the 1811 and 1812 Tavole name both spouses AND all four parents, the 1810 indice names both spouses -- and 1809, 1813, 1814, 1815, 1816 and 1817 were read groom by groom off the act head.NO DONATO MARRIAGE AT SCILLA IN NINE CONSECUTIVE YEARS, 1809 TO 1817, on either side of any act. THE OLD CONCLUSION IS RESTORED AND THE REASONING REPLACED: this archive said he was where the Scilla line stops «because registration begins in 1816», which was false; he is where it stops because nine civil years hold no Donato and their eldest son was born about 1814. A right answer that rested on a false premise is not evidence, and now it rests on nine complete readings instead.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — six Arena households at Scilla in the napoleonic years, and not one of them is Filippo's doneRow 157. Every napoleonic marriage year, 1809-1817, read for the surname ARENA on BOTH sides and in the parents' columns, which the 1811 and 1812 Tavole supply.SIX ARENA, NONE OF THEM JOINED. As a GROOM: DIEGO ARENA, sartore, 1813 act 21, son of DOMENICO ARENA, barbiere, SEVENTY -- born about 1743, the earliest Arena in this archive. As BRIDES: GIACOMA ARENA (1809 act 10) and GIOVANNA ARENA (1812 act 10, daughter of CARLO ARENA and EUGENIA ATEJA). As MOTHERS: NATALINA ARENA (1811) and ANTONIA ARENA, dead before 1811, wife of Domenico Falcomata, whose two daughters marry in 1811 and 1812. SO THERE ARE AT LEAST THREE ARENA FATHERS AT SCILLA IN THIS DECADE -- DOMENICO the barber, CARLO, and ORAZIO who is Filippo's -- AND NOTHING CONNECTS THEM. That is the finding: not a line, but the shape of a surname in a town, and a warning against the join this archive would most like to make.
20 Sep 2026Queensland BDM — the whole NICOTRA and CASSANETI/CASSANITI index, swept by surname doneRow 161. The registrar's `downloadSearch` endpoint returns a whole result set as CSV in one GET. It refuses curl with a 403 and works from inside the site's own origin. Three spellings tried.EIGHTEEN NICOTRA IN QUEENSLAND AND ONLY ONE IS OURS -- the 1963 death of «Maria Nicotra Mazza», parents JOHN NICOTRA and VENERA CASSANETI, which this archive already held. NOT ONE OTHER NICOTRA REGISTRATION NAMES EITHER OF THEM AS A PARENT, so Maria appears to have had no sibling who died or married in Queensland; the Queensland Nicotra are Maccarone, Patane, Scalia and Carbone households and none of them is hers. AND THE SURNAME IS PROBABLY SPELT WRONG: «CASSANETI» RETURNS NOTHING AT ALL in the whole index -- her own death registration is the only instance of that spelling in Queensland -- while «CASSANITI» returns five, and CASSANITI is the ordinary Catanese form. It is also the form in the PIEDIMONTE birth index, which holds a «Cassaniti Sebastiano» and a «Cassaniti Leonardo Maria» in 1875 alone. So the tree's spelling comes from an informant at a Brisbane registry in 1963, and the Sicilian records will not answer to it.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Matrimoni 1821: FILIPPO ARENA'S BROTHER doneRow 157, on its own stated method: find a sibling, and the year that sibling married will date the parents. The 1821 Tavola is alphabetised BY SURNAME, so Arena sits at the top of the book and the whole test is one crop.TWO ARENA GROOMS IN THE A SECTION, AND THE SECOND IS HIM. «ARENA GIUSEPPE x POLISTINA MARIA TERESA -- ORAZIO, e LITTERIA MORABITO -- 13 9bre», act 29. The act, read in the original: «GIUSEPPE ARENA, di anni VENTISETTE, nato in questo Comune, di professione MARINARO, FIGLIO DI ORAZIO ARENA, di professione MARINARO, E DI LITTERIA MORABITO», promessa 7 November 1821 before TOMMASO CUTELLI -- the same sindaco who married Filippo in 1815. THE METHOD PAID EXACTLY AS PREDICTED: Orazio and Litteria are named WITHOUT «fu», so both were alive on 7 November 1821, six years later than anything else showed. And LITTERIA is confirmed a third time. The other A entry, ARENA ROCCO x COSTA DOMENICA (act 20), has different parents and is a different household -- a fourth Arena family at Scilla.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — twelve Scilla marriage Tavole, 1820-1831, read at the A section doneRow 168. These Tavole are alphabetised BY SURNAME and name BOTH SPOUSES AND ALL FOUR PARENTS, so Arena sits at the top of the book and a whole year costs one crop. The only trick is that the Tavola begins at image n-3 in some years and n-2 in others, so both are fetched.EIGHT ARENA GROOMS IN SEVEN HOUSEHOLDS, AND TWO OF THEM ARE ORAZIO'S SONS. GIUSEPPE (1821 act 29) and ANTONIO (1828 act 26), both «Orazio e Litteria/Letteria Morabito» -- so Filippo has TWO brothers and the parents are dated alive to 10 AUGUST 1828. The other six: ARENA ROCCO 1821 (mother Vittoria Fiorenza); ARENA ANTONIO 1822 and ARENA LUCIANO 1827 (both «Giuseppe ed Antonia Polimeni», so brothers); ARENA CARLO 1826 («fu Antonino e Vittoria Molinari»); ARENA ANTONINO 1827 («Giuseppe e Carmela d'Amico»); ARENA BONAVENTURA 1827 («Rocco e Domenica Samari»). FOUR YEARS HAVE NO A SECTION AT ALL -- 1823, 1829, 1830, 1831 -- and 1820, 1824 and 1825 have one A entry each and it is not an Arena. THE PARENTS' COLUMN IS THE WHOLE INSTRUMENT: without it these eight men are one indistinguishable surname.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — seven more Scilla marriage Tavole, 1832-1838, and the year Orazio Arena died doneRow 168, continuing. One crop a year at the A section, except that the Tavola's first page moves between n-4 and n-2 and had to be located in four of the seven years.THE SWEEP FOUND WHAT IT WAS BUILT TO FIND. 1838 opens on «ARENA DOMENICO e POLISTINA TERESA -- FU ORAZIO, e Lett.a Morabito -- 4 7bre 1838 -- 20». THE «FU» IS THE FINDING: Orazio Arena is named LIVING in the Tavole of 1821 and 1828 and DEAD in 1838, so HE DIED BETWEEN 10 AUGUST 1828 AND 4 SEPTEMBER 1838. Letteria Morabito outlived him. And Domenico is a FOURTH SON after Filippo, Giuseppe and Antonio. TWO OTHER ARENA GROOMS, NEITHER OURS: FRANCESCO in 1833 («fu Santo, e Fiorenza Vittoria») -- which also supplies the father's name for the Rocco Arena of 1821, same mother, so they are brothers and their father is SANTO ARENA; and SANTO in 1836 («fu Domenico, e Autieri Santa»). 1832, 1834 and 1835 have one A entry each and it is not an Arena; 1837 has NO A SECTION AT ALL.

20 Sep 2026Antenati — Scilla, Morti 1830 atto 64: ORAZIO ARENA, and his parents doneRow 169, opened this afternoon with a ten-year window. The death Tavole are the same instrument as the marriage ones -- surname order, a parents column, and the SPOUSE in it for a married person -- so a year costs one A-section crop. 1828 and 1829 were read first and hold other Arena.FOUND IN THE SECOND YEAR, AND IT GIVES A GENERATION. The 1830 A section reads «ARENA ORAZIO -- Scilla -- MARINARO -- Mar.to MORABITO LETTERIA -- 12 Luglio». The act, reached by walking the dates rather than the index (the Tavola's number is its own row, not the act): «ORAZIO ARENA, MARITO LITTERIA MORABITO, nato in questo Comune, DI ANNI SETTANTANOVE, di professione MARINARO, FIGLIO DEL FU FILIPPO ARENA E DELLA FU SANTA UNGARO», dead at six in the morning of 12 July 1830 in his own house, declared by DIEGO ARENA, twenty-two, and PROSPERO ARENA, twenty-five, both marinari and both «nipote del defonto». THREE THINGS: the death is exact to the hour; he was born about 1751, NINETEEN YEARS earlier than this archive had inferred from his sons' ages; and HIS PARENTS ARE NAMED -- FILIPPO ARENA and SANTA UNGARO, generation nine. The first son of Orazio is a Filippo, born 1793: the naming custom, confirmed by a record after the fact rather than used to make an identification.
20 Sep 2026Antenati — sixteen Scilla death containers banked, 1828-1844, and the 1838 A section read partRow 170. The death volumes for the window Litteria Morabito died in. Containers fetched from inside the origin, five at a time.SIXTEEN CONTAINERS BANKED -- 1828 to 1836 and 1838 to 1844 -- so the sweep for her is a reading job. THE 1838 A SECTION IS READ AND SHE IS NOT IN IT: Arlotta Mariano, Arlotta Caterina, Arlotta Antonino, ARENA ROCCO (11 Agosto), Arlotta Mariano, Arlotta Giuseppe. AND THIS IS RECORDED AS PART, NOT DONE, FOR A REASON THIS ARCHIVE ALREADY KNOWS: a married woman can be indexed under her HUSBAND'S surname or her own. Serafina Arlotta is «ARENA Serafina, fu Giovanni» in the 1908 index. So Litteria Morabito may sit under A for Arena or under M for Morabito, and reading only the A section of a year is HALF a search. THE M SECTION HAS NOT BEEN READ FOR ANY YEAR.
21 Sep 2026The holdings table — 874 volumes across four comuni, counted not asserted doneRows 37 and 135, both written on 13 and 16 September and both asking for the same thing from different heights: which volumes exist and can be opened, and which comuni Antenati has indexed BY NAME. Built from the ark banks, which are this archive's own record of what it has listed.874 VOLUMES, 259 OPENABLE TODAY. The table is on «What Has Been Read», broken down comune by fondo by record type, with the year span, the volume count, how many have a container bought, and whether an annual index was seen. THE «BOUGHT» COLUMN IS COUNTED FROM THE BANKS RATHER THAN ASSERTED, so the page cannot overstate what this archive can open. AND A BLANK IN THE INDEX COLUMN MEANS «NOT RECORDED», NEVER «NO INDEX» -- only Scilla's restauration bank carries that column at all, which is why the figure is three and why it is not a coverage claim. THE NAME INDEX IS ON THE SAME PAGE, because where Antenati has indexed by name the volume index does not matter: Scilla deaths 1911-1943, PIZZO BIRTHS 1868-1903, Catania deaths 1911 onward -- and the ACT TYPE DIFFERS BETWEEN THEM, which is the trap. Five comuni are not indexed at all.

21 Sep 2026The instruments, written down where a reader would look doneRow 83. Nine ways of reading that this archive worked out and that were living in commit messages and memory files -- which is to say, nowhere a reader could find them.PUBLISHED ON /method/, DATA-DRIVEN FROM data/instruments.tsv. The annual Tavola; the act-head montage; the marriage processetti; the marginal annotation; the register index at s_size=500; the container fetch from inside the origin; the Riveli di anime e di terre; the Queensland BDM download endpoint; and NAA item titles. EVERY ROW CARRIES A CAUTION AND THE BUILDER REFUSES A ROW WITHOUT ONE -- an instrument described without the way it fails is how a method becomes a superstition, and the best tool here, the Tavola, changes its alphabetical key between adjacent years. THE PAGE ALSO STOPPED LYING ABOUT ITS OWN AUDIT: it said «nine were audited and five had already failed» in fixed prose while the page it links to counted for itself. 21 Sep 2026Antenati — the 1714 Piedimonte riveli index read COMPLETE, and the running totals are what made it complete doneRow 162. Container qJkjONB, 283 images. Images 1 to 17 are a summary index -- «Nomi | Somme di Lordo | di Netto | Cagione di Riveli | folio» -- alphabetised BY GIVEN NAME, so there is no shortcut to a surname and all of it had to be read.142 HOUSEHOLDS, AND NO MAZZA AND NO MAZZEO IN ANY OF THEM. THAT IS A SHAPE AND NOT A DESCENT, and it is recorded as one: a rivelo is a TAX RETURN and a household can be absent from one without being absent from the town.
21 Sep 2026The estate told about the Antenati name index doneRow 140. Not a Mazza finding but an estate one: Falco, Defranceschi and Lerena are reading Italian volumes the same way this archive was before it found the index.SENT TO THE LANDING SITE FOR CIRCULATION, carrying the query form and the three cautions that each cost something here. THE QUERY: /search-nominative/?cognome=X&localita=Y&s_size=100, which returns acts with FATHER, MOTHER AND SPOUSE already extracted. THE CAUTIONS: the ACT TYPE DIFFERS BY COMUNE -- Scilla is indexed for deaths 1911-1943 and PIZZO FOR BIRTHS 1868-1903, so carrying «deaths only» across would produce a confident negative on a series indexed the other way round; «fq=» and «facets=» are SILENTLY IGNORED and return the unfiltered count, which looks like a successful filtered query; and ONLY ROWS WITH A p.morte VALUE ARE THE DECEASED -- the rest are relatives seen from inside somebody else's act, and reading them as deaths invents people with plausible names and dates. Also that «localita=» matches any place attached to a person, not the place of registration.
21 Sep 2026The Crupi given somewhere to live on the site doneRow 77. Six documented Crupi -- Angela, her parents Francesco and Maria Vecchio, and her brothers Pietro, Antonino and Mario -- with no page, no section and no place in the four-quarters story, because they arrived through a MARRIAGE rather than a descent.
