It carries the four things this archive knows about them that the family tree does not: that «ANGELA CUPO» is ANGELA CRUPI, born 20 December 1900; that she had THREE BROTHERS IN QUEENSLAND and nobody knew, all four registrations naming FRANCESCO CRUPI and MARIA VECCHIO; that PIETRO AND ANTONINO came into Brisbane together on the OTRANTO on 19 August 1930 as consecutive entries in the aliens register, and Angela followed on the VIMINALE in 1937 with two daughters; and that ANTONINO WAS INTERNED AT LOVEDAY, a boot repairer at Ingham with the fourth finger of his right hand deformed, born at PIEDIMONTE ETNEO -- the Mazza's own town. AND THE POINT THE SECTION CLOSES ON: Pietro's missing aliens card looked like a gap and is not one. «Alien Registration Certificate NOT ISSUED», because he was naturalised in 1932. An explained absence and an unexplained one look identical until somebody checks.
21 Sep 2026Antenati name index — ALL 69 Arena deaths at Scilla 1910-1943, pulled and read doneRows 146 and 140. The query was re-run to TEST THE FORM this archive had just broadcast to three other archives -- an untested method note is untested code. It works, and it returns far more than the earlier sweep saw.SIXTY-NINE ARENA, NOT TWENTY-SIX. The earlier sweep worked from twenty-six indexed Arena deaths; the full pull returns 152 rows which deduplicate to about sixty-nine people, every one with parents or spouse. AND THE ANSWER FOR ROW 146 HOLDS AGAINST THE LARGER SET: only TWO carry «Madre Serafina Arlotta» -- ROCCO, 8 February 1932, «Padre Francesco Antonio ... Coniuge Generosa Costa», and GIOVANNI, 31 March 1936, «Padre Antonio ... Coniuge Concetta Russo». FILIPPO AND GIUSEPPE, born 1852 and 1860, ARE IN NONE OF THE SIXTY-NINE. THE «p.morte» CAUTION IS NOW CONFIRMED BY EXAMPLE, not just asserted: the index returns «Rocco Arena -- Figlio Serafina Arena -- Atto di morte 15 Agosto 1912» AND «Serafina Arena -- Morte: Scilla, 15/08/1912 -- Padre Rocco Arena, Madre Generosa Costa». The dead child is Serafina; the first row is her father seen from inside her act. Reading it as his death would have killed Rocco Arena twenty years early, with a plausible date.
21 Sep 2026Antenati name index — NICOTRA at Catania, PROSTAMO at Pizzo, CASSANITI at Catania doneThree questions the name index could in principle answer: Maria Nicotra's parents, the Prostamo who stayed at Pizzo, and the real spelling of Venera Cassaneti. EACH ZERO WAS CONTROL-TESTED in the same breath against «Arena at Scilla», which returns 69.ALL THREE ARE REAL NEGATIVES AND NOT THROTTLING. The control returned 69 risultati and 275 links; Prostamo at Pizzo and Cassaniti at Catania each returned «Nessun risultato» with thirty boilerplate links and no result count. A ZERO FROM AN OBJECT NOT VERIFIED IS NOT A NEGATIVE, IT IS NO RESULT -- the Booyzen session's phrasing, adopted. NICOTRA AT CATANIA RETURNS EIGHT DECEASED AND NOT ONE IS HERS: Concetta at Catenanuova, three Giuseppe and a Nunziata at VICOPISANO -- which is in TUSCANY. THAT IS THE «localita=» CAUTION DEMONSTRATED IN THIS ARCHIVE'S OWN DATA: the parameter matches any place attached to a person, not the place of death, so a query scoped to Catania returned people who died four hundred miles away. PROSTAMO AT PIZZO IS THE MORE SURPRISING NIL: Pizzo is name-indexed for BIRTHS 1868-1903 and this family was certainly at Pizzo -- so either the Prostamo had left by 1868 or the indexing does not reach them.
21 Sep 2026Antenati — Piedimonte Etneo, the 1871 marriage ALLEGATI: four parents certified «vivente» doneRow 6 and row 47. The marriage of Rosario Mazza and Leonarda Bonanno (Matrimoni 1871 atto 14) was read on 13 September and the act names all four parents. What had NEVER been opened is the volume beside it -- «Matrimoni, allegati» 1871, container 0noKDq9, 83 images -- so the act was re-read to calibrate the fascicolo numbering and the bundle for act 14 was found at images 43-45.THE TOP OF THE SICILIAN SPINE GETS A DATED PROOF OF LIFE, IN A PRINTED FIELD. Fascicolo 14 holds the «CERTIFICATO di seguita pubblicazione di matrimonio senza opposizione», published at the door of the Palazzo Comunale on SUNDAY 16 APRIL 1871. The form prints «figlio del ____» and «e della ____» and the clerk filled both blanks with the word VIVENTE: «figlio del VIVENTE MARIANO, professione bracciante, residente anche ivi -- e della VIVENTE MARIA CATALANO, professione industriosa, residente anche ivi», and for the bride «figlia del VIVENTE GAETANO, professione bracciante -- e della VIVENTE ANNA CAVALLARO, professione industriosa». FOUR PEOPLE AT GENERATION SIX, ALL ALIVE, ALL AT PIEDIMONTE, ALL WITH A TRADE -- and for Maria Catalano and Anna Cavallaro it is the FIRST record of a trade at all. MARIANO MAZZA is now documented twice in his own right: declaring Rosario's birth in June 1845 aged thirty-six, and named living and working in April 1871 aged about sixty-two. His death is after 16 April 1871, which is the first bound this archive has ever had on it. AND THE ALLEGATI ARE NOT PROCESSETTI, WHICH IS WORTH SAYING PLAINLY: the Bourbon-era «Matrimoni, processetti» at Piedimonte carry the spouses' BIRTH EXTRACTS and their dead parents' DEATH ACTS, and this archive has mined them. The Italian-era «Matrimoni, allegati» of 1871 carry the PUBLICATION CERTIFICATE and little else -- no birth extract, no death act. A researcher who reasons from the processetti to the allegati will plan a search that the volume cannot answer. What the allegati DO carry, and the processetti do not, is this printed vivente/fu field. AND THE HONEST COST: the marriage act itself had been read on 13 SEPTEMBER and written out in this very file, and I fetched the whole volume again -- twenty-odd images, the annual index, and a folio-by-folio calibration -- before checking. THE RULE IS THE ONE ROW 145 ALREADY WROTE, POINTING THE OTHER WAY: this archive is now large enough that BEFORE OPENING A VOLUME YOU GREP WHAT HAS BEEN READ. Two hundred and fourteen rows of searched.tsv exist so that the second search never happens, and they only work if somebody reads them.
21 Sep 2026Antenati — Piedimonte Etneo, Nati 1847 and 1848, both annual Tavole read end to end doneA TEST OF ONE NUMBER. The 1871 marriage act and the publication certificate beside it both make LEONARDA BONANNO «d'anni ventitré compiuti» on 16-30 April 1871, which puts her birth between April 1847 and April 1848. This archive holds her birth act as Piedimonte, Nati 1843 atto 101, 7 November 1843 -- which would make her twenty-seven. Either the age is wrong or there are TWO Leonarda Bonanno and the 1843 girl died. Containers for Nati 1846-1850 were already bought, so the test cost images only. Both annual Tavole read in the original at the child-name column, every entry, no sampling.THE 1843 ACT STANDS AND THE AGE IS FOUR YEARS OUT. 1847 runs 1 to 116 and 1848 runs 1 to 144 -- two hundred and sixty consecutive entries, all read -- and NEITHER YEAR HOLDS A LEONARDA BONANNO. 1847 holds no Bonanno at all; 1848 holds one, BONANNO MARIA at act 99. There is no second Leonarda, so the woman who married Rosario Mazza is the girl born 7 November 1843 and both 1871 documents understate her by four years. AND THE TWO DOCUMENTS ARE NOT TWO WITNESSES. The marriage act and the publication certificate agree on «ventitré», and the agreement is worth nothing: the same uffiziale wrote both, in the same fortnight, from the same declaration. This archive counts independent confirmations, and here is a pair that LOOKS independent -- different volume, different form, different date -- and is one statement written twice. AND THE SWEEP PAID THREE TIMES OVER, WHICH IS THE ARGUMENT FOR READING THE WHOLE TAVOLA RATHER THAN SCANNING FOR ONE NAME. (1) Nati 1847 atto 31 is «MAZZA SEBASTIANO -- Mazza Innocenzio / Cavallaro Domenica», the first child ever found for the couple in row 93, whose marriage this archive had written off as possibly unreachable. They were at Piedimonte and having children in 1847. (2) Nati 1848 atto 45 is «CASSANITI VENERA -- Cassaniti Gaetano / Venuti Maria», born the 14th -- the right name, the right town and a plausible date for MARIA NICOTRA'S MOTHER, the woman row 161 has been unable to touch. A candidate, not a conclusion. (3) A NAMESAKE TRAP CAUGHT IN THE ACT. What I first read as «Bonanno Mariano» at 1847 act 30 is «BONACCORSO Mariano», son of BONACCORSO GAETANO and Lucia Grasso -- and 1848 act 46 gives the same couple a Francesco. Piedimonte held a Gaetano Bonanno AND a Gaetano Bonaccorso at the same time, and this archive has ALREADY miscopied one for the other once, in the 1877 and 1880 birth rows of pe-mazza-acts.tsv. The zoom that corrected me is the whole method: I had read the name off a 620-pixel band and it was wrong.
21 Sep 2026Antenati — Piedimonte Etneo, Matrimoni 1871: the whole annual index read, and no NICOTRA doneRow 161, row 47, row 89. While calibrating the 1871 marriage volume the annual index was read in full -- both openings, both halves of the DUPLICATA, all twenty-six acts accounted for. The question put to it: is the marriage of GIOVANNI NICOTRA and VENERA CASSANITI here?IT IS NOT, AND THE NEGATIVE IS COMPLETE RATHER THAN PARTIAL. The 1871 «Indice Annuale per gli atti DI MATRIMONIO» names BOTH SPOUSES for every act -- groom on the upper line, bride on the lower -- and all twenty-six are legible across two openings: Monte/Cannavò 1, Previtera/Papurri 2, Pagano/Greco 3, Trovato/Spina 4, Difranceso/Cernuto 5, Messina/Parisi 6, Caltabiano/Cardile 7, Maccarone/Intelisano 8, Cavallaro/Cannavò 9, Chiavia/Gambino 10, Maugeri/Zappalardo 11, Cassanoti/Amante 12, Pennisi/Sapienza 13, MAZZA/BONANNO 14, D'Ignoto/Cassumi 15, Cipriano/Pagano 16, Papurri/Mangeri 17, Scandurra/Consolo 18, Cannavò/Caruso 19, Russo/Sanderi 20, Petrino/Ferrito 21, Russo/Fazio 22, Navarro/Costarino 23, Vecchio/Cavallaro 24, Patanè/Guarnera 25, Greco/Cardile 26. NO NICOTRA GROOM AND NO CASSANITI BRIDE. (There IS a «Cassanoti Giovanni» at act 12 -- he marries an AMANTE ROSARIA, not a Cassaniti, and the spelling is a reminder of how loosely this town's clerks handled the name.) AND THIS OPENS A ROUTE ROW 161 HAD NOT GOT. Row 161 spent fourteen consecutive years on the Piedimonte BIRTH indexes hunting Maria Nicotra and found nothing; the MARRIAGE index is a different instrument and a far cheaper one -- ONE PAGE-PAIR PER YEAR, both spouses named, no act need be opened to eliminate a year. Sweeping Piedimonte marriages 1866-1885 for a NICOTRA groom is now a bounded job of about twenty crops. Only the 1871 container is bought; the rest are one batched container lookup away.
21 Sep 2026Antenati — Piedimonte Etneo, Matrimoni 1872 atto 20: GIOVANNI NICOTRA and VENERA CASSANITI doneRows 47, 89, 161 and the brand-new row 172. The Piedimonte marriage index names BOTH spouses, one page-pair a year, so a year is eliminated without opening an act. Containers were banked for 1870 and 1872-1875 and the sweep began at 1870. 1870's N section holds one line -- «Nucifora Michele, Lo Coco Nicoletta, 15» -- and 1871's whole index of twenty-six couples had already been read clean. 1872 is the second year tried.FOUND, IN THE SECOND YEAR OF A SWEEP THAT SHOULD HAVE BEEN RUN WEEKS AGO. The N section of the 1872 annual index reads «NICOTRA GIOVANNI, E CASSANITI VENERA -- 20», and act 20 is legible whole on one page. «L'anno milleottocento settantadue, nel giorno SETTE del mese di OTTOBRE, alle ore nove antemeridiane, nella Casa Comunale ... sono comparsi GIOVANNI NICOTRA, di anni TRENTUNO, di professione bracciante, DOMICILIATO E RESIDENTE A MASCALI, figlio di ROSARIO, di professione bracciante, e di VENERA MURABITO, contadina, domiciliati e residenti PURE IN MASCALI. E VENERA CASSANITI, di anni VENTIQUATTRO, qui domiciliata e residente, figlia del FU GAETANO, e di FU MARIA CURRENTI.» Witnesses Salvadore Dadone d'Ignoto, 60, and Venerando Papurri FU Ignazio, 41, braccianti. Sindaco Barone Mariano Dottor Vocy. FOUR NEW PEOPLE AT GENERATION SIX, on the two lines this archive called the side nobody has ever searched -- ROSARIO NICOTRA and VENERA MURABITO of Mascali, both living; GAETANO CASSANITI and MARIA CURRENTI of Piedimonte, both dead. The «fu» is doing real work here and it is CONTROL-TESTED INSIDE THE ACT ITSELF: the same clerk writes «Venerando Papurri FU Ignazio» two lines above, so its absence over the Nicotra parents is a statement and not an omission. AND IT EXPLAINS FOURTEEN EMPTY YEARS. Row 161 read the Piedimonte birth indexes of 1870 through 1883 -- fourteen consecutive annual indexes, every N section quoted -- hunting Maria Nicotra, and the Nicotra are there the whole time and never produce her. THE REASON IS IN THIS ACT: her father was a MASCALI man. A wife went to her husband's comune, so the children of this marriage were registered at MASCALI, a comune this archive has never opened and never listed. The Catania hypothesis was not needed and the tree's «born Piedimonte» is her mother's town, not hers. AND THE IDENTIFICATION IS NOT CIRCULAR, WHICH MATTERS BECAUSE «Giovanni Nicotra» IS A COMMON NAME. The family's own export -- parsed long before any of this -- gives John Nicotra and Venera Cassaneti SEVEN children, and one of them is a ROSARIO NICOTRA. A first son takes the paternal grandfather's name, and the paternal grandfather in this act is Rosario. The archive held the grandson's name for years and could not have derived the grandfather's from it. Add the bride's uncommon surname, her age fitting Nati 1848 atto 45 to the year, and the province, and the join carries. ONE LOOSE END, LEFT OPEN: the 1898 marriage proclamation at Piedimonte calls Maria Nicotra's mother «MONTI VENERA». That is now a three-way conflict -- Monti, Cassaneti, Cassaniti -- and this act settles only the second and third. Row 89 keeps it.
21 Sep 2026Antenati — MASCALI listed whole, and MARIA NICOTRA'S BIRTH ACT found in the second year read doneRow 173, opened an hour earlier off the 1872 marriage act, which put Giovanni Nicotra «domiciliato e residente a MASCALI». Mascali had never been listed, never been searched and was not in any ark bank. Listed with ?localita=Mascali&s_size=500, then the annual birth indexes read at the N section -- 1873 first (the year after the marriage), then 1875 (the year the Queensland death registration implies).THE LISTING IS COMPLETE AND SAYS SO. 498 results, 498 distinct arks, and the facets read «Archivio di Stato di Catania (452)» and «Archivio di Stato di Palermo (46)» -- 452+46=498, so this is the whole holding and not a sample at the ceiling. All 498 rows are written to data/mascali-arks.tsv rather than summarised, because the listing costs a rate-limited page load and the second one is the expensive one. WHAT MASCALI HAS. Civil registration 1820-1893, the same span as Piedimonte: restaurazione 1820-1865 (Nati, Morti, Matrimoni, Nati esposti, Diversi, and from 1841 the MATRIMONI PROCESSETTI with their birth extracts), italiano 1866-1893. AND FORTY-SIX VOLUMES AT PALERMO that are not civil registration at all: RIVELI DI BENI E DI ANIME from the Tribunale del Real Patrimonio for 1616, 1625, 1636, 1652 and 1676-78, and RIVELI DI ANIME E DI TERRE from the Deputazione del Regno for 1714 and 1747-1752 in twenty-nine volumes. Piedimonte's riveli begin in 1714; MASCALI'S BEGIN IN 1616, a century earlier, and this archive has an instrument for reading them. ONE GAP RECORDED RATHER THAN SMOOTHED: the restaurazione run has NO 1844 -- it goes 1843, then 1845, in every series. 1873 CONFIRMED THE TOWN ON THE FIRST TRY. Its index is alphabetical AND carries a PATERNITA column, so a household identifies itself without opening an act. The N section: Nicotra Giovanni 23 February (padre Giuseppe), Neri Giuseppe 20 March (Salvatore), Nicotra Grazia 29 June (Alfio), Nicolosi Angelo 14 October (Giuseppe), and NICOTRA VENERA, 12 DECEMBER 1873, PATERNITA GIOVANNI -- fourteen months after the marriage, and the family's own export gives Giovanni and Venera a daughter VENERA. THEN 1875, AND IT IS HER. The 1875 index has no paternita column, so the act had to be opened. «Nicotra Maria -- 144». Nati 1875 atto 144, read in the original: «L'anno milleottocento settantacinque, addi DIECI di DICEMBRE, a ore antemeridiane otto e minuti quindici, nella Casa comunale. Avanti di me Gaetano Sacla, Sindaco ed Ufficiale dello Stato Civile del Comune di MASCALI, e comparso GIOVANNI NICOTRA, di anni ventotto, lavorante, domiciliato in Mascali, il quale mi ha dichiarato che alle ore pomeridiane NOVE e minuti TRENTA, del di OTTO del corrente mese, nella casa posta in contrada Sopra Pietrafitta al numero ..., da VENERA CASSANITI sua moglie, ambedue residenti in questo Comune di Mascali, e nata una bambina di sesso femminile che egli mi presenta, e a cui da il nome di MARIA.» Witnesses Gaetano Cardillo, 30, and Salvatore Leo, 30, both lavoranti. MARIA NICOTRA WAS BORN AT MASCALI ON 8 DECEMBER 1875 AT HALF PAST NINE IN THE EVENING. She died at Brisbane in 1963 aged eighty-eight, which the date fits exactly. The tree's «born Piedimonte Etneo» is her mother's town. ONE DISCREPANCY, RECORDED NOT HIDDEN: her father is «di anni trentuno» at his marriage in October 1872 and «di anni ventotto» here in December 1875 -- six years the wrong way in three years. Declared ages, again, and of the same kind as Leonarda Bonanno's four. THE IDENTIFICATION DOES NOT REST ON HIS AGE: it rests on the wife, VENERA CASSANITI, an uncommon pairing named in both acts, on the comune, and on a birth date that matches her Queensland death to the year.
21 Sep 2026Antenati — Mascali births 1874, 1876 and 1877 swept for the other Nicotra children partRow 173. Giovanni Nicotra and Venera Cassaniti married 7 October 1872 and the family's own export gives them SEVEN children -- Maria, Rosa, Venera, Nina, Angela, Rosario, Antonina. Two are now placed: Venera (Nati 1873) and Maria (Nati 1875 atto 144). The years either side were read at the N section, every entry.ONE TRUE NEGATIVE AND TWO YEARS THAT CANNOT BE CALLED EITHER WAY -- AND THE DIFFERENCE IS A COLUMN. 1874's index HAS a «PATERNITA» column, so the year answers the question directly. Its N section is two lines: «Neri Francesco, 1 marzo 1874, Domenico» and «NICOTRA MARIA, 28 detto, GAETANO». NO CHILD OF GIOVANNI IN 1874 -- a real negative, because the instrument names the father. AND THAT SAME LINE IS THE NAMESAKE TRAP CAUGHT IN THE ACT: Mascali registered TWO MARIA NICOTRAS EIGHTEEN MONTHS APART, one to a Gaetano in March 1874 and ours to Giovanni in December 1875. An index entry alone would have been a coin toss; opening act 144 is the only reason the right one is on the spine. 1876 AND 1877 HAVE NO PATERNITA COLUMN, and that changes the status of the reading completely. 1876's N section is five lines -- Neri Giuseppe 25, NICOTRA GIUSEPPA 81, Nicolosi Mariano 87, Nicolosi Rosario 144, Neri Lucia 170. 1877's is three -- NICOTRA MARIANO 10, NICOTRA ROSARIA 8, Nucifora Mariano 132. «Giuseppa» is not among the seven children the export names, so 1876 is probably not theirs; «ROSARIA» in 1877 may well be the ROSA the export carries. BUT NEITHER YEAR CAN BE SETTLED WITHOUT OPENING THE ACTS, and until they are opened these are READ AND UNRESOLVED -- not negatives. That is the status row 171 says this archive cannot currently write, and here are three years that need it. 1877's N section also ENDS AT THE FOOT OF ITS COLUMN, which is where this archive has produced a false negative before: the next column was checked and begins at P, so the section is complete at three entries. Acts 8 and 10 of 1877 fall within days of each other in January, so they are either twins or two households -- another reason not to guess.
21 Sep 2026Antenati name index — this archive's own nils re-tested with the suspect parameter removed doneActing on the Lerena archive's caution that a control licenses the SHAPE of a query and says nothing about the VALUE -- its own parent-pair zero survived a passing control and was false, because the mother's surname sat unparsed in a given-name field. This archive has four name-index nils on record. Each was re-run with «localita» REMOVED, and the raw page inspected rather than a count regex.THE NILS STAND, THE METHOD THAT TESTED THEM DID NOT, AND THE MEANING OF THE NILS WAS WRONG. (1) THEY ARE GENUINE. «cognome=Cassaniti» with no locality at all returns «NESSUN RISULTATO TROVATO. La tua ricerca per nome non ha prodotto risultati!» -- prose, not a count. Same for Prostamo. Musolino at Scilla still returns 7, so the form works. The extra parameter was NOT hiding anything here. (2) BUT THE DETECTOR WAS BROKEN AND GOT THE RIGHT ANSWER BY ACCIDENT. This archive reads a result count by matching «N risultati». THE ZERO PAGE DOES NOT CONTAIN THAT STRING AT ALL -- it is a 266 KB page whose count is a sentence. So «no match» from the regex was indistinguishable from a throttled page, a broken selector or a real zero, and the three were told apart only because the link count was also taken. ANY ZERO FROM THIS INSTRUMENT MUST BE CONFIRMED BY THE PRESENCE OF «Nessun risultato trovato», NOT BY THE ABSENCE OF A COUNT. (3) AND THE INTERPRETATION WAS THE REAL ERROR. «Cassaniti returns nothing» was recorded on 21 September as a real negative about this family. IT IS NOT A FACT ABOUT THE FAMILY. Within hours of that entry this archive read CASSANITI GAETANO in the Piedimonte birth Tavola of 1848 and VENERA CASSANITI in two acts -- Piedimonte Matrimoni 1872 atto 20 and Mascali Nati 1875 atto 144. The people are demonstrably in the registers and the name index returns zero for them ACROSS THE WHOLE OF ITALY. The explanation is already in this archive's own coverage notes: Piedimonte and Mascali are NOT NAME-INDEXED. So the nil means «this comune has not been indexed», not «this person is not there», and the two look identical from inside the query. THAT IS THE STATUS ROW 171 IS ABOUT, demonstrated against this archive's own data three hours after the nil was filed: an instrument was read correctly, the source was empty, and the emptiness was a fact about the INSTRUMENT.
21 Sep 2026A checker for the nils — 27 of this archive's own cannot be checked by a reader doneThe Lerena archive put its own coverage failures beside this archive's and found that «not covered» is THREE different faults rendering ONE identical empty page: at the COLLECTION (the corpus holds no parish registers at all), at the QUERY SHAPE (a compound surname exact matching cannot reach), and at a SINGLE FIELD (a value the source failed to parse, which then excludes the records that display it). Only the third is ever evidence about a family. And the part of a nil that ages well is neither the query nor the count -- it is the sentence saying WHAT THE INSTRUMENT COULD HAVE REACHED. Only one of that archive's four published nulls survived scrutiny, and it survived because its control had established the parish books were indexed and dated.WRITTEN AS tools/check_nils.py AND RUN AGAINST THIS ARCHIVE, WHICH IT DOES NOT FLATTER. Of 220 searched rows, 173 assert no nil, 20 assert a nil AND say what the instrument could reach -- AND 27 ASSERT A NIL WITH NO REACHABILITY SENTENCE AT ALL. They may every one be sound. The point is that a reader cannot tell, and neither can this archive a week later: «Trove -- Prostamo before 1954», «Find a Grave -- Prostamo and Melluso across Australia», «TNA Discovery -- every surname and town of this family», «Antenati -- Archivio di Stato di Messina, whole province». Each of those is a sentence about a family that may be a sentence about a search engine. THE CHECKER CANNOT JUDGE WHETHER THE SENTENCE IS TRUE -- only somebody who checked the coverage knows that -- so it is ADVISORY and exits 0. It makes the gap visible and finite instead of invisible and assumed empty, which is the same job check_grades.py does for dashed parent edges. It is wired into the build after check:searched. AND THE HONEST NOTE ABOUT ITS OWN ACCURACY: the nil-detecting regex is deliberately narrow and the reachability regex deliberately broad, so it will UNDER-report rather than over-report. A row it passes is not certified; a row it flags is worth a minute. AND THE NUMBER 27 WAS WRONG, WHICH IS THE POINT OF THE TOOL TURNED ON ITS AUTHOR. It was published straight out of a regex written the same hour and never tested against the rows it judges. Testing it took twenty minutes and moved it to NINE. THREE KINDS OF ERROR, ALL MINE. (1) THE NIL PATTERN MATCHED ROWS FULL OF FINDINGS: «not one» caught «NOT ONE AT NUDGEE» inside a Find a Grave row that reports ELEVEN BURIALS with plot numbers. A checker built to catch unfalsifiable claims had made one. (2) THE REACH PATTERN MISSED PLAIN ENGLISH: three rows state their boundary perfectly -- «BT 26 and BT 27 cover UK PORTS ONLY», «CIVIL REGISTRATION IN THE PROVINCE OF MESSINA BEGINS IN 1820», «this family moved Italy to Australia and never through Britain» -- and were flagged because the word «coverage» was absent. The fix was to look for a BOUNDARY of place, date or record type rather than for a vocabulary. (3) AND I THEN WROTE THAT IT WOULD «UNDER-REPORT RATHER THAN OVER-REPORT», WHICH WAS BACKWARDS. It over-reported by three to one. THREE ROWS HAVE SINCE BEEN GIVEN THE SENTENCE THEY LACKED, and two of them needed it badly: the Piedimonte birth-index sweeps for Maria Nicotra now say outright that the instrument could only ever reach children registered at Piedimonte, and she was registered at MASCALI. NINE REMAIN.
21 Sep 2026Antenati — Mascali, Nati 1841 and 1847: Giovanni Nicotra is in neither partRow 173. Giovanni Nicotra gave TWO ages three years apart -- «di anni trentuno» at his marriage on 7 October 1872 (born about 1841) and «di anni ventotto» at his daughter Maria's birth on 10 December 1875 (born about 1847). His own birth act would settle which, and would carry his parents ROSARIO NICOTRA and VENERA MURABITO independently of the marriage act. Both candidate years were read at Mascali. THE INSTRUMENT: the Bourbon-era «Tavola Alfabetica annuale de' Nati», alphabetical by surname, with columns Num d'ordine, Cognomi e Nomi de' NATI, PATRIA, Cognomi e Nomi de' GENITORI, Giorno della Nascita. Nicolosi Domenico -- Nicolosi D. Francesco / Mercurio D.a Francesca -- 3 Gennaio 1847» and «69. NICOTRA MARIA -- Nicotra Alfio / Sudani Carmela -- 23 Luglio 1847». Then an empty O section, then P. It establishes that NO GIOVANNI NICOTRA WAS REGISTERED AT MASCALI IN 1841 OR 1847 -- the only two years his own two statements point to. It does NOT establish that he was born elsewhere: this archive has now watched the same man lose six years between two acts, and Leonarda Bonanno lose four between a birth act and a marriage, so a declared age is a bracket and not a date. THE NEXT STEP IS THE REST OF THE BRACKET: Mascali Nati 1838-1850, ten more annual Tavole, each one crop. THE REACH, STATED: these are Mascali's own birth registers for those two years, read at the only section that could hold him, in a table that names both parents. If he was born in another comune -- and his wife's household was at Piedimonte, eight kilometres away -- no Mascali Tavola will ever hold him. AND ONE NAME WORTH REMEMBERING: NICOTRA ALFIO fathered a Maria at Mascali in 1847, and an ALFIO NICOTRA fathered the 1875 Piedimonte Maria Nicotra who is NOT ours. Not pursued, not claimed.
21 Sep 2026The Atlas layers checked — a ramp in the wrong order, a colour decided by row order, and Zambrone missing doneA peer session added three Atlas layers to this repository (commit 80ce21e, tools/atlas.py and site/src/pages/atlas.astro, nothing else touched) and explicitly left one judgement to this archive. The commit was pulled and read rather than accepted: the diff, the ramp, the join key, and every count it asserts.THE WORK IS SOUND AND THREE THINGS WERE WRONG. (1) THE RAMP WAS IN THE WRONG ORDER, which was the judgement offered. It had `route` as WORSE than `partial`. build_coverage.py's own docstring defines `route` as «reachable, but through a different archive» and `partial` as «the listing itself is incomplete, so NOTHING IS SETTLED», and the rows bear it out: `route` is Piedimonte past 1893, waiting in the Tribunale's duplicate for Catania, named and findable; `partial` is Briatico, EIGHTY-ONE ROWS HELD AGAINST FIVE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-FIVE AVAILABLE, where nobody knows what is in the rest. A known errand beats not knowing what you are looking at. Swapped, and both labels rewritten to say which is which. (2) A BUG NEITHER OF US HAD LOOKED FOR, AND IT IS THIS ARCHIVE'S OWN FAVOURITE SHAPE. A comune has one coverage row PER SERIES and they disagree -- Piedimonte has SIX `open` and one `route`. The code assigned with `=`, so the colour was decided by WHICHEVER ROW SAT LAST IN THE FILE. It rendered correctly for all seven comuni on the day it was written, and would have turned Piedimonte green the moment somebody appended an `open` row beneath its `route` one. A silent change of meaning caused by the ORDER OF A DATA FILE. Fixed to take the WORST status, ranked explicitly, and TESTED BY REVERSING coverage.json AND RE-RUNNING: byte-identical output, seven comuni unchanged. (3) ZAMBRONE IS NOT ON THE MAP AT ALL, which is why twelve of thirteen coverage rows matched and not thirteen. The reason is structural and bigger than the Atlas: THE MAP'S PLACES COME FROM THE EXPORT'S PEOPLE, and Zambrone holds no exported person. Its people -- the whole Ventrice line -- exist only in documented-additions.tsv, which now runs to 115 rows and includes every person this archive has added from a register: Mariano Mazza, Maria Catalano, Rosario Nicotra, Venera Murabito, Gaetano Cassaniti, Maria Currenti. ALL OF THEM ARE INVISIBLE ON THE ATLAS. The counts the peer asserted were checked and are right: 18 of 20 burials land on a drawn place, the two that do not are Arneytown, and the coverage join is on the same `head()` the roster uses, which work-list 158 says is the thing that silently drops people when it changes.
21 Sep 2026The reversal test run across this archive's own builders — one real fault, in the questions doneThe Atlas fold bug found yesterday was a many-to-one join written with `=`, invisible because it rendered correctly on the day it was written. The peer session that introduced it checked its own estate for more of the class and found none. THIS ARCHIVE OWED ITSELF THE SAME CHECK, and by hand rather than by eye: tools/check_order.py reverses each data/*.tsv, re-runs the builder that reads it, and compares the output.EIGHT BUILDER/INPUT PAIRS TESTED, ONE REAL FAULT, AND IT WAS IN THE QUESTIONS. build_questions.py numbered each question with `enumerate(rows, start=1)` -- THE ROW'S POSITION -- and the kit RENDERS that number, as «{r.n ?? i + 1}». So inserting a question anywhere but the end silently renumbered every question below it, and anyone who cited «question 7» was pointing at a different question the next day. Nothing had gone wrong yet and nothing would have looked wrong when it did. FIXED THE WAY THE WORK LIST ALREADY DOES IT: `n` is now an explicit column in questions.tsv, assigned once and never reused, and the builder REFUSES a row whose `n` is missing, non-numeric or duplicated -- both refusals tested by breaking the file on purpose. All eight pairs now give identical output on reversed input. AND THE TEST ITSELF HAD TO BE FIXED TWICE BEFORE IT WAS WORTH TRUSTING. (1) It read the source file INSIDE `open(src, "w")`, which truncates first, so it got an empty file and died; the restore in `finally` is the only reason that cost nothing. (2) FAR MORE IMPORTANT, IT FIRST COMPARED BYTES, and six of eight pairs «failed» because most of these builders emit a LIST in file order -- reversing the input reverses the list, the bytes differ, and every one of those six was benign. A BYTE COMPARISON CANNOT TELL PRESENTATION FROM DATA LOSS. The fault worth catching is a fold in which one row overwrites another, and that shows up as a CHANGED VALUE or a VANISHED KEY, never as a reordering. The test now canonicalises both outputs by sorting every list by its own contents and compares the SET OF FACTS. Six false alarms became one true one. NOT WIRED INTO `npm run build`, deliberately: it temporarily rewrites tracked data files and restores them in a `finally`, which survives an exception but not a kill, and a build killed mid-check would leave a reversed data file looking like an edit. It is `npm run check:order`, run on purpose. AND A QUESTION WENT GREEN ON THE WAY PAST: «Where was Maria Nicotra born?» had been open since the archive was built and was answered this morning -- MASCALI, Nati 1875 atto 144. Four open now, not five.
21 Sep 2026Antenati — Mascali, the hunt for Rosario Nicotra's household: Matrimoni 1840 clean, and a torn Tavola partRow 173, reaching for GENERATION SEVEN. Rosario Nicotra and Venera Murabito are known only from inside their son's marriage act of 1872. Their OWN marriage would name all four of THEIR parents, because Mascali's restauration marriage Tavola is alphabetical by groom and carries a four-line parents column -- groom's father, groom's mother, bride's father, bride's mother -- with the dead marked «fu». One Tavola read per year. Giovanni was born about 1841 or 1847 on his own two statements, so the marriage brackets to roughly 1830-1845.1840 IS A CLEAN NEGATIVE AND THE METHOD IS PROVED. The Tavola of Matrimoni 1840 is alphabetical, names both spouses and ALL FOUR PARENTS, and its N section is ONE ENTRY: «10. Nicolosi Francesco / Mercurio Francesca -- Nicolosi Domenico, Mercurio Angela, Mercurio Pietro, Bassi Maria». The section's end was checked on the facing page, which opens at P. NO NICOTRA MARRIAGE AT MASCALI IN 1840. AND A CHANGE OF PLAN WORTH RECORDING. Hunting one marriage across fifteen years is the expensive way; the BIRTH Tavole name both parents too, so a single «Nicotra [X] -- Nicotra Rosario / Murabito Venera» would place the household AND bracket the marriage from below. That is the sweep to run, and its containers are banked: Mascali Nati 1839, 1840, 1841, 1842, 1843, 1845, 1846, 1847, 1848, plus Matrimoni 1840 -- FIFTEEN MASCALI CONTAINERS NOW HELD, so the remaining work is reading rather than rate-limited fetching. AND ONE PHYSICAL CONDITION THAT IS NOT A NEGATIVE. THE 1842 BIRTH TAVOLA IS TORN, and the archive's own card says so: «Torn Page(s)». The surviving leaf carries the R and S sections with the head of the page lost, and the M-to-P run sits on the damaged side. 1842 CANNOT BE READ AS A NEGATIVE FOR NICOTRA -- the section that would hold them is missing, not empty. Recorded as a condition of the volume rather than a result, which is the distinction row 171 exists for. THE REACH, STATED: 1840's marriage Tavola is Mascali's own, complete, alphabetical, and names four parents a couple; it can only ever answer for marriages celebrated AT MASCALI in that year. If Rosario Nicotra married in his wife's comune, no Mascali Tavola will hold it.
21 Sep 2026Antenati — Mascali births 1839–1848, every N section read: the only Nicotra at Mascali is ALFIO'S doneRow 173. Giovanni Nicotra is «domiciliato e residente a MASCALI» at his marriage in 1872 and his parents ROSARIO NICOTRA and VENERA MURABITO are «residenti pure in Mascali», yet he is in neither of the two years his own declared ages point to. The question this sweep puts: WAS ROSARIO'S HOUSEHOLD AT MASCALI AT ALL in the 1840s? The instrument answers it directly, because Mascali's Bourbon-era «Tavola Alfabetica annuale de' Nati» is alphabetical by surname and NAMES BOTH PARENTS -- so a single «Nicotra [X] -- Nicotra Rosario / Murabito Venera» would settle it without an act being opened. Every surviving year of the decade was read at the N section, and in each the section's END was checked into the next letter before anything was written down.NINE YEARS, EIGHT READABLE, AND ROSARIO NICOTRA'S HOUSEHOLD IS IN NONE OF THEM. 1839: THERE IS NO N SECTION AT ALL -- M (Musumeci, Marano, Maccarrone, Mangano, Mercurio, Marchese, Mannino) runs straight into P (Patane), so not one child of any N surname was born at Mascali that year. 1840: three entries -- NICOTRA VENERA (padre ALFIO, madre Carmela Sudieri, 11 May), Nicolosi Mariancola (Nicolosi Francesco / Mercurio Gioacchina), Nicolosi Leonardo (Nicolosi Salvadore / Finocchiaro Venera). 1841: one entry, Nicolosi Salvadore (Nicolosi Francesco / Mercurio Francesca, 24 December). 1842: THE VOLUME IS TORN and the archive's own card says so; the surviving leaf carries R and S with the head of the page lost and the M-to-P run on the damaged side. NOT READABLE, AND NOT A NEGATIVE. 1843: the N section is HEADED AND COMPLETELY EMPTY, and so is the O beneath it. 1845: one entry, Nicolosi Giovanna (Nicolosi Salvadore / Finocchiaro Venera, 22 April). 1846: N HEADED AND EMPTY again. 1847: two entries, Nicolosi Domenico and NICOTRA MARIA (padre ALFIO, madre Sudani Carmela, 23 July). 1848: one entry, Neri Antonina (Neri Leonardo / Borzi Maria, 11 July). (1844 does not exist in this series at all -- the gap recorded when Mascali was listed.) SO THE ONLY NICOTRA HOUSEHOLD AT MASCALI IN THAT DECADE IS ALFIO NICOTRA AND CARMELA SUDIERI, who appear in 1840 and again in 1847. ROSARIO NICOTRA AND VENERA MURABITO ARE ABSENT FROM EVERY READABLE YEAR. WHAT THAT MEANS, STATED CAREFULLY. It does NOT mean they are not the people named in the 1872 act -- that act is read and unambiguous. It means THEY WERE NOT AT MASCALI IN THE 1840s, and therefore GIOVANNI NICOTRA WAS ALMOST CERTAINLY NOT BORN THERE. The household came to Mascali between about 1848 and 1872. His birthplace is a comune this archive has not yet identified, and the way to it is his own marriage ALLEGATI -- the 1872 publication certificate, which in this province prints the spouses' birthplaces -- not another Mascali sweep. THE REACH, STATED: these are Mascali's own birth registers for nine consecutive years, read at the only section that could hold the surname, in a table that names both parents. They can answer for Mascali and for nothing else. AND ALFIO NICOTRA IS WORTH A LINE: an ALFIO NICOTRA also fathered the 1875 Piedimonte Maria Nicotra who is NOT ours. Whether the Mascali Alfio and the Piedimonte Alfio are one man is untested and nothing is claimed.
21 Sep 2026Antenati — the 1872 marriage allegati: GIOVANNI NICOTRA WAS BORN AT GIARRE doneNine consecutive Mascali birth Tavole, 1839-1848, put Rosario Nicotra's household nowhere in the town, so Giovanni was not born there and the question became WHERE. The instrument was already proved this morning on Rosario Mazza: the Italian-era «Matrimoni, allegati» hold a printed «CERTIFICATO DI SEGUITA PUBBLICAZIONE DI MATRIMONIO» which, in the 1872 form, prints «nato a ____» SEPARATELY FROM «residente a ____» for each spouse. Piedimonte's 1872 allegati, container LDmQaWG, 85 images.«NATO A GIARRE», AND A SIXTH TOWN ENTERS THE ARCHIVE. The bundle for act 20 is at images 71-74, found by reading a neighbouring certificate rather than by hunting wrapper numbers -- image 65 is fascicolo 19, «Salvadore Scipelliti (nato a BRONTE) e Antonia Zumbo (nata a Piedimonte Etneo)», which matches the annual index entry for act 19 and fixes the count. THE CERTIFICATE, read in the original: «TRA GIOVANNI NICOTRA -- NATO A GIARRE -- residente a MASCALI -- di professione bracciante -- di eta anni TRENTUNO -- figlio di ROSARIO, di professione bracciante, residente a Mascali, e di VENERA MURABITO, di professione contadina, residente a Mascali. E VENERA CASSANITI -- NATA A PIEDIMONTE ETNEO -- residente a Piedimonte Etneo -- di professione contadina -- di eta anni VENTIQUATTRO -- figlia di FU GAETANO e di FU MARIA CURRENTI.» Published at Mascali, «Fatto oggi in Mascali il di CINQUE del mese di SETTEMBRE dell'anno 1872», signed Di Leonardo -- the groom's own comune, as the code required. THE WHOLE SHAPE OF THE SICILIAN QUARTER IS NOW VISIBLE: Giovanni Nicotra born at GIARRE about 1841, living at MASCALI by 1872 with his parents; Venera Cassaniti born at PIEDIMONTE ETNEO in 1848; married at Piedimonte 7 October 1872; their daughter MARIA born at MASCALI 8 December 1875; Maria marries Salvatore Mazza of Piedimonte about 1900 and dies at Brisbane in 1963. FOUR COMUNI IN ONE WOMAN'S DESCENT, and the family tree recorded one of them. AND IT EXPLAINS THE NINE EMPTY MASCALI YEARS EXACTLY: the household came from Giarre, which is the next comune down the coast.
21 Sep 2026Antenati — GIARRE listed whole, and s_size=500 is a PAGE, not a ceiling doneGiarre had never been listed. The first query, ?localita=Giarre&s_size=500, returned 500 arks and a facet total of 808 -- the exact trap this archive's own note warns about, a listing sitting at the ceiling and therefore a SAMPLE rather than a holding.AND THE CEILING TURNS OUT NOT TO BE ONE. «&s_page=2» returns the remaining 308, and 500+308 = 808 = the facet total exactly. THIS ARCHIVE'S STANDING NOTE WAS WRONG: s_size=500 is a PAGE SIZE, not a limit on what can be retrieved, and any holding larger than 500 is reachable in two loads instead of being permanently a sample. THE CORRECTION MATTERS BEYOND GIARRE: Briatico is recorded in antenati-coverage.tsv as «THE WORST UNDER-LISTING IN THE ARCHIVE: eighty-one rows held against 555 available», and that under-listing was accepted as a wall. IT IS NOT A WALL. It is two page loads. GIARRE'S HOLDING, banked whole in data/giarre-arks.tsv: 808 volumes, ALL AT CATANIA, 1820-1873, and it is a bigger comune than any other in this archive -- 164 Nati volumes, 147 Morti, 131 Matrimoni, 44 Matrimoni processetti, 42 Matrimoni allegati, plus Cittadinanze and their allegati. IT HOLDS TWO COPIES OF EVERY YEAR -- e.g. Nati 1841 as an_ua68999 (segnatura 15941) and an_ua19993253 (segnatura 16345) -- so a damaged or missing leaf in one may survive in the other, which is a resource this archive has not had anywhere else. AND UNLIKE MASCALI IT HAS 1844. FIRST YEAR READ: Nati 1841, container 5dZj97v, 226 images, annual Tavola alphabetical at the back. ITS M SECTION RUNS STRAIGHT INTO O -- THERE IS NO N SECTION AT ALL -- so no Nicotra was born at Giarre in 1841. (A row I first read as «Nicolosi Salvadore» at 207 is MIGLIERINO Salvadore, still in M; corrected at full width before anything was written down.) His two declared ages differ by six years, so the window 1838-1848 must be swept, not the single year.
21 Sep 2026Antenati — GIARRE births 1838–1848, every N section read: the only Nicotra there is a DON partRow 177. The 1872 publication certificate says Giovanni Nicotra was «NATO A GIARRE», and his two declared ages differ by six years, so the window is a bracket and not a year. Eleven consecutive annual Tavole read at the N section — 1838 to 1848, every year the series holds. Giarre's Tavola is alphabetical and NAMES BOTH PARENTS, so a Nicotra of a ROSARIO would identify itself without an act being opened.ROSARIO NICOTRA'S HOUSEHOLD IS IN NONE OF THE ELEVEN YEARS. The N section is ABSENT ALTOGETHER in 1838, 1840, 1841, 1842 and 1845 — the alphabet runs M straight into O or P. It is WRITTEN AND LEFT EMPTY in 1839, 1843 and 1844, which proves the clerks did write the heading when they had anything to put under it. Three years hold one entry each: 1846 «NICOTRA SALVADORE — Giarre — Nicotra D.r D. ALFIO / CONSOLI D.na ANTONINA — 5 Novembre»; 1847 «NICOLOSI Giuseppe — Nicolosi Angelo / Leotta Gaetana — 31 Gennaio»; and 1848 «110. NICOTRA SALVATORE — ivi — Nicotra D.r D. ALFIO / CONSOLI D.na ANTONINA — 2 Gennaro». THE ONLY NICOTRA HOUSEHOLD AT GIARRE IN ELEVEN YEARS IS A TITLED ONE — «Dottor Don» Alfio Nicotra and «Donna» Antonina Consoli — and they name a Salvatore twice, fourteen months apart, which is what a family does when the first child dies. ROSARIO NICOTRA IS A BRACCIANTE in his son's marriage act. Different household, different class. AND A STRUCTURAL FAULT FOUND IN THE PAPER, WHICH IS WHY THIS ROW IS «part» AND NOT «done». The Osservazioni column of the 1848 N entry reads, in the clerk's own hand, «IN FINE DELL'ALFABETO RIPORTATA ALTRA VOLTA LA LETTERA M» — at the end of the alphabet the letter M is entered a second time. GIARRE'S TAVOLE APPEND EXTRA LETTER-SECTIONS AFTER Z, for acts registered late. 1848 appends F and M; 1841 appends F and B; 1842 and 1843 append others still. SO «THE ALPHABET REACHED Z» IS NOT THE END OF THE INDEX, and a negative taken from the main run alone is incomplete. FIVE TAILS HAVE BEEN CHECKED AND ARE CLEAN of any second N — 1838, 1839, 1840, 1841 and 1848. SIX HAVE NOT: 1842, 1843, 1844, 1845, 1846 and 1847. Until those six tails are read this is eleven years swept and five years finished, and it is recorded that way. THE REACH, STATED: these are Giarre's own birth registers for eleven consecutive years, read at the only section that could hold the surname, in a table that names both parents. They answer for Giarre and for nothing else. AND ONE NAME KEEPS RECURRING: ALFIO. The only Nicotra household at MASCALI in 1839-1848 is Alfio Nicotra's; the only one at GIARRE is Dr Don Alfio Nicotra's; and an ALFIO NICOTRA fathered the 1875 Piedimonte Maria Nicotra who is NOT ours. Three Alfio Nicotras within eight kilometres. Nothing is claimed from it.
21 Sep 2026Antenati — the six remaining Giarre tails read: the 1838–1848 sweep is COMPLETE doneThe Giarre sweep was filed «part» because the 1848 index says in its own Osservazioni column «IN FINE DELL'ALFABETO RIPORTATA ALTRA VOLTA LA LETTERA M» -- these Tavole APPEND EXTRA LETTER-SECTIONS AFTER Z for acts registered late, so reaching Z is not reaching the end of the index. Five tails had been checked; six had not. All six are now read: 1842, 1843, 1844, 1845, 1846 and 1847.NOT ONE OF THE SIX CARRIES AN APPENDED N, AND THE SWEEP IS FINISHED. The appendices are real and they are various: 1842 appends T, P, B and F across images 223-224; 1843 appends R, M, C, G and T across 240-242; 1844 appends a single G on its last image; 1845 appends F and T; AND TWO YEARS APPEND NOTHING AT ALL -- 1846 ends at Z (Zappala Benedetta, Alfio, Giuseppe) with a blank facing page, and 1847 ends at Z with two further Zappala on the last leaf. SO THE APPENDIX IS A HABIT AND NOT A RULE, which is itself worth knowing: its absence in a volume is not evidence that the reading is complete, because 1846 and 1847 look identical to a volume whose appendix simply has not been reached. ALL ELEVEN YEARS ARE NOW VERIFIED IN BOTH THE MAIN RUN AND THE TAIL: 1838, 1839, 1840, 1841, 1842, 1843, 1844, 1845, 1846, 1847, 1848. ROSARIO NICOTRA'S HOUSEHOLD IS IN NONE OF THEM, and the only Nicotra at Giarre in eleven years remains «Nicotra D.r D. ALFIO» and «Consoli D.na Antonina», who name a Salvatore in November 1846 and again in January 1848. WHAT THIS LEAVES, STATED PLAINLY. Giovanni Nicotra's own marriage certificate says «NATO A GIARRE». Giarre's own registers, read end to end for eleven years, do not hold him. THREE READINGS SURVIVE AND NONE IS PROVED. (1) BOTH HIS DECLARED AGES ARE WRONG and he was born outside 1838-1848 -- he is «trentuno» in 1872 and «ventotto» in 1875, already six years apart, so a third error is not a stretch; the window either side is 1833-1837 and 1849-1853. (2) HE WAS BORN IN GIARRE'S TERRITORY BUT REGISTERED ELSEWHERE. RIPOSTO WAS PART OF GIARRE AND SEPARATED FROM IT IN THIS EXACT PERIOD; a man born in the lower town could call himself «nato a Giarre» and be entered in Riposto's register. THIS IS A HYPOTHESIS FROM GENERAL HISTORY, NOT FROM A DOCUMENT, and the way to test it is to list Riposto on Antenati and read its N sections. (3) «Nato a Giarre» is loose for one of the frazioni -- Macchia, Trepunti, Altarello, San Giovanni Montebello. THE REACH, STATED: this is Giarre's own birth register, every surviving year 1838-1848, read at the only section that could hold the surname, main run AND appendix, in a table that names both parents. It answers for the comune of Giarre and for nothing else.
21 Sep 2026Antenati — RIPOSTO attempted, and the control caught a throttle pretending to be a zero not-yetThe Giarre sweep finished clean across eleven years, so Giovanni Nicotra's «nato a Giarre» needs another register. RIPOSTO was part of Giarre and separated from it in this period, so a man from the lower town could say «nato a Giarre» and be entered at Riposto. THE SESSION IS RATE-LIMITED, and «Riposto returns nothing» would have been a fact about the throttle. This is the archive's own 520-byte signature, and it is the third distinct shape of false zero met today: the name index whose zero is prose and not a count; the listing that sits at the s_size ceiling and looks complete; and now a 403 that parses as an empty result set. ALL THREE RENDER AS «no results». RIPOSTO IS UNTESTED. Not negative, not a wall -- untested, pending the throttle clearing (this archive's own note gives it twenty minutes or more).
21 Sep 2026Antenati — the false-zero test the Lerena archive asked for, run four ways doneThe Lerena archive withdrew its own advice: it had told this archive that confirming the POSITIVE assertion of emptiness was the reliable discriminator, and then FamilySearch printed «Historical Record Search Results (0)» and «No Results Found» for every query for an afternoon because its session had expired -- stably, across two reads, after a ninety-second wait, with a control that had passed that morning. Its sharper rules: a control must be RECENT and ARITHMETICALLY IMPOSSIBLE TO BE ZERO; ask a SECOND search path on the same source; and when a null looks like a discovery, suspect it hardest. It asked whether the «Nessun risultato trovato» check holds for Antenati in the signed-out state, because that is the state in which the check has to work. FOUR PROBES, ONE MINUTE APART.THE CHECK HOLDS FOR ANTENATI, AND THE REASON IS STRUCTURAL: THERE IS NO SESSION TO EXPIRE. (1) ARITHMETICALLY-IMPOSSIBLE CONTROL: localita=Catania returns «25476 risultati» and 500 arks. A provincial capital cannot be zero, and it was not. (2) TRUE-ZERO SIGNATURE, established deliberately rather than assumed: localita=Zzqqxxwv -- a locality that cannot exist -- returns HTTP 200, a 266 KB page, ZERO arks and «Nessun risultato trovato» in prose. That is what an Antenati nil looks like. (3) THE THROTTLE SIGNATURE, already known and confirmed again: 520 bytes, title «403 Forbidden», no arks, NO «Nessun risultato» -- which is exactly how the Riposto query failed earlier today. The two are distinguishable by SIZE ALONE, 266 KB against 520 bytes. (4) SIGNED-OUT: the same Riposto query run with credentials OMITTED returns byte-identical output to the signed-in one -- 421 risultati, 421 arks. ANTENATI'S REGISTER SEARCH IS PUBLIC AND STATELESS, so there is no cookie to lose and no signed-out state that differs. The Lerena failure mode cannot occur here. WHAT THIS ARCHIVE ADOPTS ANYWAY: the arithmetic control. Every nil re-tested today was controlled against «Arena at Scilla» or «Mascali», both of which merely OUGHT to return something. «Catania» cannot be zero, and that is a stronger guard for nothing extra.

IT DOES NOT SAY WHICH LINE IS DISPUTED, because corrections.tsv carries prose and not a field name, and inferring which fact a paragraph is about is the kind of guess this archive refuses.
22 Sep 2026Antenati — Riposto births: seven years read, THREE Nicotra households, and none of them Rosario's partRow 177. Giovanni Nicotra's certificate says «nato a GIARRE» and eleven Giarre years do not hold him; RIPOSTO registers separately from Giarre from 1821 and is the only other candidate. Its Tavola is alphabetical and NAMES BOTH PARENTS. Seven years now read at the N section, each checked into the next letter.SEVEN YEARS, THREE NICOTRA HOUSEHOLDS, AND ROSARIO IS IN NONE OF THEM. 1838: one entry, «170. NICOTRA MARIA», father FRANCESCO. 1840: the alphabet runs M straight into P, NO N SECTION. 1841: likewise no N section -- and its last leaf is TORN, so that year is not a finished negative. 1842: no N section. 1846: the N heading is WRITTEN AND LEFT EMPTY, and so is the O beneath it. 1847: TWO entries, both Nicotra -- «154. Nicotra Rosa, NICOTRA GIUSEPPE / Autieri Angela, 17 Nov» and «152. Nicotra Rosa, NICOTRA SALVADORE / Barbagallo Maria, 15 do». 1848: one entry, «166. NICOTRA LEONARDO, Riposto, Nicotra Giuseppe / Autieri Angela, 26 Dicembre» -- the same household as the year before. SO RIPOSTO HOLDS AT LEAST THREE ORDINARY NICOTRA HOUSEHOLDS in the window: GIUSEPPE x ANGELA AUTIERI (1847 and 1848), SALVADORE x MARIA BARBAGALLO (1847), and FRANCESCO (1838). THAT IS THE POINT OF THE TOWN. Giarre's only Nicotra across ELEVEN years is a «Dottor Don» with a «Donna» wife; Mascali's only one across nine years is an Alfio. Riposto has three in seven, all of them the sort of family a bracciante marries into -- and still not the one this archive wants. STILL UNREAD: 1839, 1843, 1844 and 1845, and the torn tail of 1841. Their containers could not be fetched: the origin returned the 520-byte «403 Forbidden» signature four times across two attempts, which is the throttle and not a result. THE IMAGES ARE NEVER THROTTLED, only the HTML that carries a container id -- so this is a wait, not a wall. AND ONE ROUTE NOT YET TRIED THAT MAY NOT NEED THEM: Riposto's 1841 births exist in a SECOND VOLUME, «17517, suppl. 2» (an_ua73235), which may carry the leaf the first volume has lost.

Nothing matches that.

The questions each of these would answer →

The other archives

The Mazza Archive is one of seven, each following a different line of the same family. They use the same method and the same five words for how sure a thing is.

  • The Defranceschi Archive Istria · Kvarner · Carnia A family written down under eight sovereigns, in four languages, and spelled at least nine different ways. Defranceschi · De Franceschi · Defranceski · Defrančeski
  • The Falco Archive Arienzo, near Naples Nine documented generations born, married and buried in one valley, and then a migrant ship to Brisbane. Falco · Zampiello · Morgillo · Crisci · Liguori · Maione · Rivetti · Annecchino
  • The Blažević Archive Senj and the Krmpotska mountains An evidence-first archive for the family of Hedviga Blažević of Senj — carriers, a tobacco factory, and a wall grave. Blažević · Papić · Vukelić · Žubrinić · Kalanj
  • The Booyzen Archive Graaff-Reinet · Cradock · Johannesburg A Cape Dutch family that kept marrying British women, changed one letter of its name in a war, and came off the train at Johannesburg with nothing. Booyzen · Booysen · Kolbe · Barry · Downing · van Jaarsveld · Schoeman
  • The Lerena Archive Rosario · the Cape · the Rand A horse-dealer out of Argentina, a stable under Table Mountain, and four family traditions tested against the record rather than repeated. Lerena · Taylor
  • The D'Arcy Archive England and Ireland · Parramatta · Brisbane Out with the 39th Regiment in 1825, and four generations afterwards in two Brisbane cemeteries. D'Arcy · Saniger · Sneyd · Hornby · Wakefield · Atkinson · Murdoch

And for children: Our Family — The same ten families, told for a six-year-old.

An evidence-first archive of the Mazza family of Piedimonte Etneo in Sicily — and of Briatico and Scilla in Calabria, of New York and Buenos Aires, and of Brisbane. Every claim carries its confidence, or is marked as unproven. Living people are named — name and relationship only, never a date, a place, a photograph or a record.
